Thus the selected construction in\nSections3--5 applies to every possible old-cut survivor in(1).\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), with12/5<lambda<17/7. The actual t is\n(C-N)/(1+v)-1. Since N<=9/2, C>=C0, and1+v<=lambda,\n\n    t >= (C0-9/2)/lambda-1\n       >277/850>3/10. Hence (G_B)_t<2-92*(3/10)<0.\nWith t held fixed, both expressions have C derivative-2Ct^2<0.\n\nIncrease1+v to lambda and decrease C to C0 in these algebraic\nexpressions, holding u,w fixed. Both changes decrease t, which stays\nabove3/10. They can only increase max(G_A,G_B). (20)\n\nThe closed virtual endpoint u=2 is permissible in the displayed\nalgebraic functions and their continuous extensions; the actual\ncertificate survivors already have u<2.\n\n## 7. From(20),\n\n    lambda G_A=(C0-N)J0-(Hc-N)N^2\n                         -(C0^2/lambda)(Hc-N)^2. All finite dimensions,\nzero and unbounded steps, and one static function per run remain allowed.\nNothing below is an upper bound on the unrestricted W_n.\n\nThe accepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 (row convention and normalization),\nb98bb807d514e80d (complete K and original benchmark definitions),\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a (complete ray channel), and dfde2fa326c908bf (complex/lens\nchannel). C101--C104 are retrieval evidence, not correctness predecessors.\nAll new ingredients used below are derived within this joint document.\n\nMAIN CONCLUSION. For the entire legal family (1)--(6), on every original\nH_m checkpoint pair with enclosing-m parameters unchanged, let V_m^obt\nbe the supremum of the exact true-distance payoff. Then\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) V_m^obt/K_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=13/10,\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) V_m^obt<=13/[5 exp(1)]<1. (0)\n\nHere the COMPLETE same-word K equals COMPLETE F_ray and is dominated\nby both full Huber-augmented complex/lens references. Section6 gives\nthe stronger finite factor133613/134064<1 for all parameters in one\nstructural pair class. Appendix C prices ALL remaining pairs, including\npositive eta and small E, and proves the uniform asymptotic statement.\nAn obtuse instance might improve a reference by less than the bound;\nwe do not claim V_m^obt<=K. The whole family nevertheless misses the\noriginal profile by a fixed relative asymptotic amount. This is an\nadversary-family obstruction, not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.\n\n## 1. Exact declared family and every physical support test\n\nFix the ORIGINAL word h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2). Put\n\n    S_j=sum block_j, T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2,\n    A=b-1>0, E=d-1>0, sigma=2N-1.\n\nBlocks contain their actual chronological physical steps, including zeros.\nThese letters are local abbreviations, not the original B_n benchmark.\nChoose independent u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,beta>=0,kappa>=0,w>0,ell>0. Set\n\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha, H=Mv+beta,\n    p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(-w,ell),\n    a_q=u^2+v^2, a_r=w^2+ell^2, c=<q,r>=uw-v ell,\n    z=(Eu-alpha,Ev-beta), eta=w alpha-ell beta,\n    R_q=u alpha+v beta-kappa, G=R_q-eta.\n\nHere eta is the r-plane height at the ending middle proximal point;\nit is not a free reserve. Define one static finite affine maximum\n\n    J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},\n    F(x)=min_y {J(y)+||x-y||^2/2}, X_0=(T+delta,-H).       (1)\n\nThe complete endpoint tests for the stated constant gradients p,q,r are\n\n    delta>=0, vH<=(1+u)delta+kappa, R_q>=0;              (2)\n    G>=0, G+(M-1)(a_q-c)>=0;                            (3)\n    <r,z>-N a_r>=0;                                    (4)\n    <r-q,z>+kappa-(a_r-c)>=0,\n    <r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c)>=0;                          (5)\n    <r-p,z>-N(a_r+w)>=0.                               (6)\n\nUnlike the positive-quadrant case, (3) is not automatic, and the rate\na_r-c in (5) is not presumed positive. Both actual endpoints are kept.\nThe prefix r comparison is automatic: at its proximal point\ny=(delta+S_0-t,-H), the p-minus-r margin is\n(1+w)(delta+S_0-t)+ell H>0 for every physical t in[0,S_0].\nThe p-versus-zero and p-versus-q margins are minimized at t=S_0;\ntheir values are exactly the first two conditions in (2).\n\nCheckpoint b sends the iterate to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At middle\ncumulative mass t in[0,S_1] its proposed proximal point is\n\n    y=(-alpha,-beta)+(S_1-t)q.\n\nThe q-versus-zero margin is R_q+(S_1-t)a_q>=0. Its margin over p\nis nonnegative because p's height is nonpositive. Its margin over r\nis G+(S_1-t)(a_q-c), so (3) proves every physical middle query.\nThe second checkpoint sends the iterate to z. At tail cumulative\nmass t in[0,S_2], put k=1+t, so y=z-k r with1<=k<=N. The r-minus-zero\nmargin has positive loss rate a_r; the r-minus-p margin has positive\nloss rate a_r+w. Their final tests are (4),(6). The r-minus-q margin\nhas rate a_r-c of unrestricted sign, so (5) proves every physical tail\nquery. This includes the actual terminal point.\n\nThese tests are necessary as well as sufficient for the specified\ngradients of (1). A subgradient of a finite affine maximum is a convex\ncombination of its active slopes: otherwise a separating direction\ncontradicts its directional derivative, the maximum active directional\nslope. The slopes p,q,r are uniquely exposed by directions(1,0),(0,-1),\n(0,1), respectively. Hence their occurrence as subgradients requires\ntheir respective planes to be active. Evaluating activity at the stated\nendpoints gives exactly (2)--(6).\n\n## 2. Static regularity and the actual nearest minimizer\n\nJ is continuous, finite, convex and nonnegative. Its proximal objective\nis coercive and strictly convex, hence has a unique minimizer y(x).\nComparison with y+t(v-y), convexity and t decreasing to0 show\ng(x)=x-y(x) belongs to the subdifferential of J at y(x). Conversely,\nthat subgradient inequality and the quadratic expansion prove proximal\noptimality. Adding the subgradient inequalities at two points gives\n||g(x)-g(x')||^2<=<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>. In particular g is1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluating the proximal objective at the old minimizer gives the upper\nfirst-order bound for F. Applying its subgradient inequality at the new\nminimizer and completing the quadratic gives the lower bound. Thus\n\n    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.\n\nThis proves that F is convex, differentiable and1-smooth with gradient g.\nIts minimum is0 and its minimum set is exactly\n\n    K={x_1<=0, -w x_1+ell x_2<=0, -u x_1-v x_2<=kappa}. (7)\n\nIndeed J=0 gives F=0, and an attained zero proximal objective forces\nboth nonnegative terms to vanish. The geometry in (7) has been rederived\nfor w>0: it lies below the line x_2=(w/ell)x_1<=0 and above\nx_2=(-kappa-u x_1)/v>=-kappa/v. It is a triangle when kappa>0 and\nthe singleton0 when kappa=0. Its left vertex has\nx_1=-kappa/(u+vw/ell); the denominator is positive even when u=0.\n\nSince T+delta>0 and H>0, the closest point to X_0 is\n\n    t_star=min(H,kappa/v), x_star=(0,-t_star),\n    D=(H-kappa/v)_+,\n    R^2=dist(X_0,K)^2=(T+delta)^2+D^2.                  (8)\n\nProof: every point of K has first coordinate<=0 and second coordinate\n>=-kappa/v. These separate lower bounds on squared coordinate distances\nare simultaneously attained at the displayed point, which satisfies all\nthree inequalities of (7). This includes both positive-part branches.\nHere R denotes this local true distance, not the project's ratio in W=2R.\n\n## 3. All directed rows, optimum slacks, payoff and holding price\n\nAssign slopes0,p,q,r affine costs0,0,kappa,0, respectively. At any of\nthe physical samples, activity at y_i=x_i-g_i gives\n\n    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j<=0.                     (9)\n\nThe last inequality is precisely j-plane activity against i's plane.\nThus every orientation, all remote physical pairs, and repeated/zero-step\nqueries satisfy the project convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\nInclude x_star with slope, cost and value0. Its zero plane is active,\nso (9) also proves both optimum orientations. No interpolation row is\ndeleted and no Gram-only realization is substituted for the function.\n\nThe ending-query affine reserves are delta,R_q,L_0, with\n\n    L_0=<r,z>-N a_r,\n    L_p=<r-p,z>-N(a_r+w),\n    L_q=<r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c).\n\nThe optimum slacks k_i-<g_i,x_star> are0,kappa-v t_star,ell t_star.\nThey are nonnegative and are distinct from the affine reserves.\nThe exact true-distance payoff is\n\n    V=2F(X_n)/R^2=P/R^2,\n    P=2<r,z>-sigma a_r\n     =2eta-2E c-sigma a_r>0.                           (10)\n\nIts positivity follows from (4): P=2L_0+a_r>=a_r>0.\nReplacing F by L F for arbitrary L>0 keeps the same trajectory under\nthe original steps h_i/L and the same normalized payoff.\n\nFor an appended nonnegative list of total mass tau, the SAME function\nholds gradient r at every appended physical query exactly while\n\n    0<=tau<=min{L_0/a_r, L_p/(a_r+w),\n               L_q/(a_r-c) if a_r>c, otherwise +infinity}. (11)\n\nThe three margins become L_0-tau a_r, L_p-tau(a_r+w),\nL_q-tau(a_r-c). A nonpositive loss rate imposes no upper limit.\nExposedness of r supplies necessity. During this exact interval\nV(tau)=V(0)-2tau a_r/R^2. No reserve by itself licenses continuation.\n\n## 4. Positive residual is paid twice; a conditional complete comparison\n\nPut eta_+=max(eta,0). From R_q>=0 and G>=0 one has\n\n    kappa<=u alpha+v beta-eta_+.\n\nCombining this with prefix condition (2) gives\n\n    Mv^2+eta_+<=delta+u(A-Mu),\n    D>=(Mv-u alpha/v+eta_+/v)_+.                        (12)\n\nThe first inequality charges the residual to prefix activity; the second\ncharges it to the true radius. Define only for an UPPER RELAXATION\n\n    v_hat=sqrt(v^2+eta_+/M).\n\nThen M v_hat^2<=delta+u(A-Mu), and\n\n    R^2>=(T+delta)^2+(M v_hat-u alpha/v_hat)_+^2.        (13)\n\nFor (13), the positive-part numerator M v_hat^2-u alpha is the same\non both sides; its actual denominator v is no larger than v_hat.\nThis does not assert that changing v yields a legal new trajectory.\n\nFor T>=2A, the full-norm calculation proved in Appendix A gives\n\n    (u^2+v_hat^2)/R^2<=C(T,A,M),\n    C(T,A,M)=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.             (14)\n\nCompleting the terminal squares, with their actual signs retained,\n\n    P=E^2(u^2+v^2)/sigma+2eta\n      -sigma(ell-Ev/sigma)^2\n      -(sqrt(sigma)w+Eu/sqrt(sigma))^2.                 (15)\n\nTherefore the ENTIRE legal obtuse parameter family on a word satisfying\nT>=2A obeys\n\n    V<=max{E^2/sigma,2M} C(T,A,M).                      (16)\n\nMore sharply, if eta<=0 OR E^2>=2M sigma, then\n\n    V<= E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma.                            (17)\n\nIn the second case,2eta_+<=E^2 eta_+/(M sigma); in the first case\nthe extra term is nonpositive. These are explicit domain restrictions,\nnot assertions about all obtuse endpoints. Appendix B proves directly\nthat on every ORIGINAL H_m pair,m>=128, the right side of (17) is at\nmost(61473/68840)K_n(H_m), with complete K=F_ray and the original-B\nlimsup at most2(61473/68840)/exp(1)<1. Thus (17) closes a substantial\ndeclared subdomain, but (16) alone does not close its complement.\n\nA second upper bound, valid for EVERY legal obtuse tuple when T>=2A,\nis obtained by dropping the negative terms -2Euw,-2ell beta and\ncompleting w and ell separately:\n\n    V<= [A^2/T^2+E^2 C(T,A,M)]/sigma.                   (18)\n\nHere alpha<=A and R>=T, and (14) with eta_+=0 follows from the weaker\nconditions kappa<=u alpha+v beta. Again this is an upper relaxation.\n\n## 5. Middle-to-terminal transport: the third price cannot be removed\n\nEvaluate the retained q-plane margin at the actual terminal point:\n\n    L_q=E(a_q-c)-N(a_r-c)-G>=0.\n\nTogether with G>=0 this gives the necessary inequality\n\n    N a_r<=(E) a_q+(N-E)c.                             (19)\n\nWhen e=E/N<=1, Cauchy--Schwarz implies ||r||<=||q||: setting\nt=||r||/||q||>0 in (19) gives t^2<=(1-e)t+e, or\n(t-1)(t+e)<=0. More specifically, direct coordinate factorization gives\n\n    (w-u)(w+e u)+(ell+v)(ell-e v)<=0.                 (20)\n\nIf w>u, it follows that ell<e v and\n\n    (w-u)(w+e u)<=(ell+v)(e v-ell)<=e v^2,             (21)\n\nthe last inequality using e<=1. These are necessary whole-function\nconditions from BOTH directions of the physical q,r rows. Keeping only\nthe middle row permits false states and misses (19).\n\nHere is a quantitative reserve bound using this transport. Joint proof of the full-norm inequality\n\nThe following calculation is included in full and rechecked for the\nrelaxed variables used in Section4. It is not an invocation of C104 as a fact.\nEquation labels in this appendix carry the prefix A.\n\n\nWe prove from scratch: for A,M,T>0 with T>=2A, u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,\n\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha>=0,\n    Mv^2<=delta+u(A-Mu),\n\none has\n\n    (u^2+v^2)/[(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2]\n       <=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.                 (A15)\n\nThis controls the horizontal contribution that the former orthogonal\nargument did not need. It includes both nearest-minimizer branches.\n\nFirst suppose M<=A+2A^2/T. Put P=A-Mu and d=delta, so0<=d<=P.\nThe constraint gives M(u^2+v^2)<=Au+d. Direct expansion yields\n\n    A^2(T+d)^2-M T^2(Au+d)\n       =A P T^2+(2A^2T-MT^2)d+A^2d^2>=0.              (A16)\n\nIf the coefficient of d is nonnegative, every term is nonnegative.\nIf it is negative, substitute d<=P in just that term; the result is\nat least P[(A-M)T^2+2A^2T]+A^2d^2>=0 by this regime's bound on M.\nDropping the second nonnegative radius square now proves the first\nalternative A^2/(M^2T^2) in (A15). No saturation or endpoint limit is\nneeded in this regime.\n\nSuppose next M>A+2A^2/T. Then M>A. For fixed u,alpha put\n\n    P=A-Mu, d=P-alpha, k=1+u, c=uP,\n    x=v^2, c_0=u alpha/M, H_0=T+d.\n\nLegal v>0 forces P>0. The ratio on the left of (A15) is\n\n    (u^2+x)/[H_0^2+M^2(x-c_0)_+^2/x].                  (A17)\n\nOn x<=c_0 it increases. On x>=c_0, differentiation has the sign of\n\n    H_0^2-2M^2c_0-M^2u^2\n      +2M^2c_0^2/x+M^2u^2c_0^2/x^2.\n\nThe first three terms are at least\n\n    T^2-2Mu(A-Mu)-M^2u^2\n       =T^2-A^2+(A-Mu)^2>=0.\n\nThe branches agree at x=c_0. Therefore (A17) is bounded by its value\nat the allowed endpoint x=(d+c)/M. There\nMx-u alpha=(1+u)d>=0, giving the bound\n\n    F_u(d)=(Au+d)/\n       { M[(T+d)^2+M(1+u)^2d^2/(d+c)] }, 0<=d<=P.     (A18)\n\nThis saturation is only an upper relaxation; no final activity is assumed.\n\nWrite b_0=Au. For u>0, c>0, differentiation of (A18) has the sign of\n\n    G_u(d)=(T+d)(T-d-2b_0)-M(1+u)^2 H_u(d),\n    H_u(d)=d[(b_0+c)d+2b_0c]/(d+c)^2.\n\nExplicitly\n\n    H_u'(d)=2c^2(d+b_0)/(d+c)^3>=0,\n    G_u'(d)=-2(d+b_0)-M(1+u)^2H_u'(d)<=0,\n    G_u(P)=(T+A)(T-A-2Au).                            (A19)\n\nThe last identity follows by substituting P=A-Mu and d+c=P(1+u):\nthe subtracted term at P is Mu(A+P+2Au), which combined with the\nfirst product leaves exactly the displayed factorization.\n\nIn the present regime\n\n    u<=A/M<T/(T+2A)<=(T-A)/(2A).\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent to (T-2A)(T+A)>=0. Hence\nG_u(P)>=0. Because G_u is nonincreasing, G_u(d)>=G_u(P)>=0 throughout\n[0,P]; thus F_u(d) is nondecreasing. When u=0, its formula reduces to\nd/{M[(T+d)^2+Md]}, whose derivative has the sign T^2-d^2>0 for\n0<d<=A. Its limit at d=0 is0. These observations also handle all\nrelaxed endpoint degeneracies; the actual constraint always has v>0.\n\nIt follows that (A18) is at most its alpha=0 value at d=P. Writing\nt=P in[0,A], that value is\n\n    [A^2+(M-A)t]/[M^2(T^2+(2T+A+M)t)].                 (A20)\n\nThis is a quotient of positive affine functions of t. Its derivative\nhas the sign of\n\n    (T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)].\n\nIf nonpositive, the maximum is its value at t=0, namely A^2/(M^2T^2).\nIf nonnegative, the maximum is at t=A and equals\nA/{M[(T+A)^2+MA]}. In the latter case\n\n    M[(T+A)^2+MA]-A(T+A+M)^2\n       =(T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0,\n\nso the value at t=A is at most1/(T+A+M)^2. This proves (A15).\nEvery relaxation, both radius branches, and the u=0 and boundary cases\nhave been retained. This is exactly the full-norm inequality required in Section4 of this joint draft.\n\n\n## Appendix B. Its values are exactly\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, and direct substitution gives\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                        (3)\n\nNotice the normal is indexed by j, not i. Every ordered row, including\nthe optimum sample x_*=g_*=f_*=0, follows from the displayed normal\ninequality. In particular Q_(i,*)=0.\n\nFor ANY trajectory of (2), the distance telescope gives\n\n 2sum h_i f_i=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2\n                       +sum h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2. Two distinct tight global cuts prohibit a full certificate\n\nFor this section assume every physical step is strictly positive. Define\nS_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, P_j=1+S_j and D_j=1+s-S_(j+1). For h_j>1 the interval\nK=[-eta,1], eta=(h_j-1)/D_j, and initial point x_0=P_j give the following\nexact projection trajectory: gradients1 through query j, followed by\ngradients -eta through query n. Query j is1 and query n is -eta.\nIndeed the bridge lands at -(h_j-1)=-D_j eta, and every subsequent\npositive step moves toward -eta while staying on the negative plateau.\nEquation(4) gives\n\n V_h=P_j^2-A^2(h_j-1)^2/D_j^2.\n\nConsequently every full certificate satisfies\n\n A(h_j-1)_+ <= P_j D_j.                                    (5)\n\nCall j a tight global cut if h_j>1 and equality holds in (5).\nThe following assertion concerns full certificates, not an unproved\nequivalence between primitive functions and dual certificates:\n\n A strictly positive word with an original full certificate has\n AT MOST ONE tight global cut.                              (6)\n\nHere is a full-coordinate proof. A projection interval containing a\npositive plateau, with initial point1+s, produces all gradients equal\nto1 and V_h=Q_ij=0. Equation(1) and PSD imply M_prim e=0.\nExpanding its i-th row for i<n gives\n\n sum_l Lambda_(i,l)(S_l-S_i)=2h_i(1+S_i).                   (7)\n\nFor completeness, T e has entries1+S_i. At a nonterminal index i,\n(T^T L_flow^T e)_i=-2h_i+2h_i S_(i+1). Combining half this quantity\nwith D_prim,ii gives h_i(1+S_i), which proves (7).\n\nAt a tight cut j the interval trajectory above has V_h=0. Its gradient\ncolumn and e span the two block indicators, so the indicator p of\n{0,...,j} is in ker M_prim. Complementarity follows directly from(1):\nthe left side is a sum of nonpositive Lambda_ij Q_ij and the right side\nis nonnegative, so each strict negative Q forces its multiplier to0.\nLet the two query blocks be I={0,...,j}, J={j+1,...,n}. Formula(3) gives\n\n Q_(i,l)=-(1+eta)eta sum_(t=l)^(n-1)h_t, i in I,l in J,\n Q_(l,i)=-(1+eta)sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, l in J,i in I.\n\nStrict positivity of the steps therefore allows cross edges only\nI->n and J->j. All other cross multipliers are0 as a consequence,\nnot an imposed sparsity assumption.\n\nAt the terminal row of M_prim p=0, direct use of its full matrix gives\n\n (h_j-1)Lambda_(n,j)=sum_(i<=j)Lambda_(i,n).                 (8)\n\nIn fact (Tp)_n=S_(j+1), (Tp)_j=1+S_j, all other possible row-n targets\nwithin J have the same Tp value, and (T^T L_flow^T p)_n is minus the\nsum on the right. Its D_prim term is0.\n\nIf Lambda_(n,j)=0, (8) and nonnegativity remove EVERY edge from I to J.\nThe row i=j of(7) would then have a nonpositive left side, because all\nremaining targets have S_l<=S_j, and the strictly positive right side\n2h_j(1+S_j). This is impossible. Thus Lambda_(n,j)>0.\n\nIf k>j were another tight cut, its own exact interval transcript has\n\n Q_(n,j)=-(1+eta_k)sum_(t=j)^(k-1)h_t<0.\n\nComplementarity at that second cut forces Lambda_(n,j)=0, a contradiction.\nThis proves(6) without deleting a formal coordinate or fixing a remainder.\nNo version of(6) for zero-step words is used below; the explicit negative\nfunctions in Sections5--8 also handle the zero-step endpoints we state.\n\n## 3. What changed, what is closed, and the next interface\n\nThe old full-primitive reports650089feeda25479 and504a69a1e000d6aa\ndescribed bounded numerical failures, including one length5 search.\nThey did not supply the explicit families or exact exclusions above.\nEarlier saturated-cut drafts, including e59fe188876aa98d and\nf7cee3034df8cb7f, already derived a much fuller single-cut multiplier\nextraction. Original certificate and explicit projection functions\n\nFor a nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put s=sum h_i and A=1+s.\nThe original formal gradients are g_0,...,g_n, with\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_n||^2.\n\nAn original full certificate has ALL n(n+1) off-diagonal multipliers\nLambda_ij>=0, zero diagonal, e=(1,...,1),\n\n T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),\n M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(n+1).\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 Section10 gives the exact identity\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.       (1)\n\nThus a single actual smooth convex trajectory with V_h<0 excludes every\nsuch array. No necessity or dual-attainment theorem for primitive energy\nis needed for this implication. No support or gradient coordinate is\nremoved, including at a zero step.\n\nWe use the following elementary construction and prove its properties.\nFor a compact convex set K containing0 in a finite-dimensional space, set\n\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                         (2)\n\nCompleting the square shows that its unique maximizing g is P_K(x),\nthe Euclidean projection. Its normal inequality is\n\n <x-P_K(x),v-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K.\n\nAdding the normal inequalities at x and y gives\n||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, hence P_K is1-Lipschitz.\nThe two maximizing inequalities give, for an increment t,\n\n 0<=F_K(x+t)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),t>\n       <=<P_K(x+t)-P_K(x),t><=||t||^2.\n\nTherefore F_K is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.\nIt has F_K(0)=0=min F_K. If0 is in the interior of K, any x!=0 admits\na sufficiently small positive multiple epsilon*x in K; substituting it\nin (2) proves F_K(x)>0, so0 is the unique minimizer.\n\nAt a projection transcript put y_i=x_i-g_i. Its values are exactly\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, and direct substitution gives\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                        (3)\n\nNotice the normal is indexed by j, not i. Every ordered row, including\nthe optimum sample x_*=g_*=f_*=0, follows from the displayed normal\ninequality. In particular Q_(i,*)=0.\n\nFor ANY trajectory of (2), the distance telescope gives\n\n 2sum h_i f_i=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2\n                       +sum h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2.                             (4)\n\nThe identity uses actual values and gradients. It holds even when the\nlast point is outside K or a step is zero.\n\n## 2. Two distinct tight global cuts prohibit a full certificate\n\nFor this section assume every physical step is strictly positive. Define\nS_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, P_j=1+S_j and D_j=1+s-S_(j+1). For h_j>1 the interval\nK=[-eta,1], eta=(h_j-1)/D_j, and initial point x_0=P_j give the following\nexact projection trajectory: gradients1 through query j, followed by\ngradients -eta through query n. Query j is1 and query n is -eta.\nIndeed the bridge lands at -(h_j-1)=-D_j eta, and every subsequent\npositive step moves toward -eta while staying on the negative plateau.\nEquation(4) gives\n\n V_h=P_j^2-A^2(h_j-1)^2/D_j^2.\n\nConsequently every full certificate satisfies\n\n A(h_j-1)_+ <= P_j D_j.                                    (5)\n\nCall j a tight global cut if h_j>1 and equality holds in (5).\nThe following assertion concerns full certificates, not an unproved\nequivalence between primitive functions and dual certificates:\n\n A strictly positive word with an original full certificate has\n AT MOST ONE tight global cut.                              (6)\n\nHere is a full-coordinate proof. A projection interval containing a\npositive plateau, with initial point1+s, produces all gradients equal\nto1 and V_h=Q_ij=0. Equation(1) and PSD imply M_prim e=0.\nExpanding its i-th row for i<n gives\n\n sum_l Lambda_(i,l)(S_l-S_i)=2h_i(1+S_i).                   (7)\n\nFor completeness, T e has entries1+S_i. At a nonterminal index i,\n(T^T L_flow^T e)_i=-2h_i+2h_i S_(i+1). Combining half this quantity\nwith D_prim,ii gives h_i(1+S_i), which proves (7).\n\nAt a tight cut j the interval trajectory above has V_h=0. Its gradient\ncolumn and e span the two block indicators, so the indicator p of\n{0,...,j} is in ker M_prim. Complementarity follows directly from(1):\nthe left side is a sum of nonpositive Lambda_ij Q_ij and the right side\nis nonnegative, so each strict negative Q forces its multiplier to0.\nLet the two query blocks be I={0,...,j}, J={j+1,...,n}. Formula(3) gives\n\n Q_(i,l)=-(1+eta)eta sum_(t=l)^(n-1)h_t, i in I,l in J,\n Q_(l,i)=-(1+eta)sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, l in J,i in I.\n\nStrict positivity of the steps therefore allows cross edges only\nI->n and J->j. All other cross multipliers are0 as a consequence,\nnot an imposed sparsity assumption.\n\nAt the terminal row of M_prim p=0, direct use of its full matrix gives\n\n (h_j-1)Lambda_(n,j)=sum_(i<=j)Lambda_(i,n).                 (8)\n\nIn fact (Tp)_n=S_(j+1), (Tp)_j=1+S_j, all other possible row-n targets\nwithin J have the same Tp value, and (T^T L_flow^T p)_n is minus the\nsum on the right. Its D_prim term is0.\n\nIf Lambda_(n,j)=0, (8) and nonnegativity remove EVERY edge from I to J.\nThe row i=j of(7) would then have a nonpositive left side, because all\nremaining targets have S_l<=S_j, and the strictly positive right side\n2h_j(1+S_j). This is impossible. Thus Lambda_(n,j)>0.\n\nIf k>j were another tight cut, its own exact interval transcript has\n\n Q_(n,j)=-(1+eta_k)sum_(t=j)^(k-1)h_t<0.\n\nComplementarity at that second cut forces Lambda_(n,j)=0, a contradiction.\nThis proves(6) without deleting a formal coordinate or fixing a remainder.\nNo version of(6) for zero-step words is used below; the explicit negative\nfunctions in Sections5--8 also handle the zero-step endpoints we state.\n\n## 3. One triangle construction for both physical lengths\n\nUse the following two exact parameter sets:\n\n A   a    R     P       Tbar    b        H       gamma   mu      nu\n 10  3/2  5/2   4       15/2    5/2      1       1/5     3/8     2/3\n 13  3/2  5/2   130/31  21/2    167/62   112/31  5/31    21/52   16/21\n\nHere R=1+a, P=a+b, Tbar=A-R, H is the total of the middle steps, and\n\n d=b-1=P-R,\n P=2AR/(A+R),\n gamma=d/Tbar=P/(2A)=R/(A+R),\n mu=d/P=Tbar/(2A),\n nu=(Tbar-R)/Tbar.\n\nFor A=10 the middle block consists of the single step1. (18)\n\nThe single function is F=F_K from(2), with initial point\n\n X_0=A w+P u+Tbar v.                                     (19)\n\nIt depends on A and epsilon. For A=13 it is independent of c.\n\nTo verify the minimum, put\nbeta=mu+gamma*epsilon-(nu-mu)*epsilon^2 and\nalpha=gamma-(nu+beta)*epsilon. Then w=-alpha u-beta v.\nBoth parameter sets obey\n\n 5/31<=gamma<=1/5,\n 3/8<=mu<=21/52,\n 2/3<=nu<=16/21,\n 0<nu-mu<1.\n\nFor epsilon<=1/20 these imply\n\n mu<=beta<=21/52+1/100<1/2,\n alpha>=5/31-53/840=2557/26040>0,\n alpha<=1/5<3/8<=beta.\n\nThus0=alpha*p+(beta-alpha)*q+(1-beta)*w is a strictly positive\nconvex combination. Every physical query and every directed interpolation row\n\nThe prescribed gradient pattern is\n\n h5:    p,p,q,q,w,w;\n h6(c): p,p,q,q,q,w,w. Furthermore\n\n <q,u>=-gamma-(1-nu)epsilon<0,\n <q,v>=(1-mu)(1+epsilon^2)>0.\n\nThus Y_k+tau_i q is in the full q cone for EVERY allowed tau_i,\nincluding zero middle steps. Finally both <w,u> and <w,v> are negative,\nso a*w is in the full w cone, and the zero normal is in it as well.\n\nThese checks prove G_i=P_K(X_i) at EVERY original query. They give a\nsingle actual static function with the exact GD updates in(20).\nFormula(3) now proves Q_ij<=0 for ALL ordered indices i,j in\n{*,0,...,n}, not only adjacent or chosen support pairs. The exact values\nare F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2; no independent repricing is involved.\n\n## 7. Strict negative primitive energy\n\nThe terminal point and gradient both equal w. Then\n\n ||z'-z||<=(1+s)e_n+2|s'-s|\n          <40000eta_box=1/2500,\n ||z||<13.\n\nEquation(4) holds for the perturbed trajectory as well, even though its\nterminal point need not equal its gradient. {"id": "f2d25059720eb51d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T19:23:35.799551+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max44 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED closure of the NEW positive correlated-tail law on ORIGINAL H_m: all explicit PSD vectors, every original Q row and the true minimum distance are proved, but the ENTIRE family has exact supremum 1/(1+2 sum H_m) for every m>=32. Arbitrary physical tail coefficients, nonnegative innovation slack, embeddings and finite orthogonal sums are included. Exact original-B limit is Z_1/(2 exp(1))<41209/50000. A smallest-level checkpoint deletion plus separate-affinity reduction closes the paid family; fixed-terminal-parameter deletion is explicitly refuted on the SAME words.", "evidence": "# Positive correlated tails on the original ruler: exact family closure\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission is\nauthorized. This is max's first round of the new two-round allocation:\ncompleted baseline 43, proposed completed count after return 44, ceiling\n45, attempted ceiling 46 including the historical interruption. Returning\nthis manuscript is not evidence that the outer loop has already exited.\n\nThe original all-word, all-sufficiently-large-horizon Goal remains OPEN.\nThe normalization throughout is W=2R: at smoothness one the payoff is\ntwice the final objective gap divided by the squared distance from the\ninitial point to the ENTIRE minimizer set.\n\nAccepted inputs are 1252b8823bb5a290 for the original interpolation\nconvention, b98bb807d514e80d for K and the original benchmark,\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a for the COMPLETE alternating-ray reference,\ndfde2fa326c908bf for the FULL schedule-dependent complex reference,\nand the UNCONDITIONAL denominator-ray assertion of 04219554e41d9750.\nNo conditional seed existence is assumed. No earlier unverified family\nclosure is a mathematical premise. All new ingredients below are proved\ntogether, rather than promoted to facts and reused without a gate.\n\n## 1. New state, explicit data, and result\n\nFor any physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) with every h_i>1, set\nd_i=h_i-1. Choose eta>=1, tail coefficients t_i in [0,1], and innovation\nslacks sigma_i>=0. These are explicit scalar inputs, not a Gram or\nfunction-value feasibility oracle. Use the orthonormal basis\ne_0,...,e_n of R^(n+1). Starting at\n\n    g_n=e_n,  x_n=eta e_n,  q_n=1,  delta_n=eta-1,\n\nconstruct backward, for i=n-1,...,0, by\n\n    q_i=t_i q_(i+1)+(1-t_i)(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))/d_i+sigma_i,\n    v_i^2=q_i-t_i^2 q_(i+1),\n    g_i=t_i g_(i+1)+v_i e_i,\n    x_i=x_(i+1)+h_i g_i.                                      (1)\n\nHere v_i is the NONNEGATIVE square root. The reserve delta_i will be\ncomputed by (2) below; it is <x_i,g_i>-q_i. Thus every scalar in (1)\nis obtained by a finite specified recursion before the next index is\nconstructed. No sample is discarded or re-indexed.\n\nDefine the one static function for this full run by\n\n    C=conv{0,g_0,...,g_n},\n    F(x)=max_(v in C) {<x,v>-||v||^2/2}.\n\nThe new dependence is an overlapping future gradient plus a fresh\northogonal innovation at each physical step. For example,\nt_i=beta_m and sigma_i=0 at every i gives a genuinely changing,\npositively correlated law on the original H_m below. Its dimension\nand number of listed gradient vertices grow with m. Every coordinate\nthat survives several steps can change amplitude at all those steps.\nThis is not a common added gradient or an orthogonal sum of old\ntriangle constructions.\n\nThis draft proves that ALL choices in (1) are legal, with exact payoff\n\n    V=(2eta-1)/r_0,  r_0=||x_0||^2\n      =dist(x_0,argmin F)^2.\n\nNevertheless, for the ORIGINAL enclosing-m parameters\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,\n    c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1,\n\nthe entire declared family has, for every m>=32, exactly\n\n    sup_(eta,t,sigma) V = 1/(1+2 sum H_m).                     (T)\n\nIn particular this new positive-correlation law cannot improve on\nHuber, the complete same-word K, or the complete same-word ray channel.\nThe exact original-B normalization and full-complex comparison are in\nSection 8. The limiting family/B ratio is strictly below one.\n\nThe law leaves the nonpositive-adjacent-correlation hypotheses of\nC98/C100. It also permits short-step reserves larger than the C99 cap:\nalready the all-t=1 choice has delta_i/q_i equal to a whole remaining\nmass plus eta-1. Thus the new closure is not obtained by renaming a\npreviously excluded negative-correlation or capped-reserve law.\n\n## 2. Scalar identities and actual positive semidefinite geometry\n\nSuppose the suffix has been constructed. Its points and gradients lie\nin span{e_(i+1),...,e_n}. Put q=q_(i+1), delta=delta_(i+1), t=t_i,\nd=d_i, h=1+d, and sigma=sigma_i. Then\n\n    v_i^2=(1-t)[t q+(delta+q)/d]+sigma >=0.\n\nThe vectors in (1), not merely their pairwise proposed correlations,\ntherefore give a real positive semidefinite Gram matrix. Taking inner\nproducts in x_i=x_(i+1)+h g_i gives\n\n    delta_i = delta+(1+d t)q+d sigma,                         (2)\n    r_i = r_(i+1)+h^2 q_i+2h t(delta+q),                     (3)\n\nwhere r_i=||x_i||^2. These identities also prove delta_i>=0 and q_i>0\nat every index. All dimensions are physical dimensions, not radii.\n\nFor i<j, orthogonality of the innovations gives\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=(product_(k=i)^(j-1) t_k) q_j >=0.              (4)\n\nAlso q_i>=t_i q_(i+1). Repeated use of this inequality implies\n\n    <g_i,g_j><=q_i  for every i<j.                            (5)\n\nFor the concrete all-t=beta_m pilot, 0<t_i<1 and v_i>0 at every\nphysical index. Its gradients are all distinct, and adjacent inner\nproducts are strictly positive. This statement follows symbolically\nfrom (1), without evaluating any instance.\n\n## 3. Every original ordered row, including the remote rows\n\nLet y_i=x_i-g_i. We prove that\n\n    <y_i,g_j-g_i><=0 for every i,j in {0,...,n},\n    <y_i,-g_i><=0.                                          (6)\n\nThe second inequality is -delta_i<=0. The adjacent forward comparison\nhas the exact value\n\n    <y_i,g_(i+1)-g_i>\n      =(1-t_i)(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))\n        +d_i(t_i q_(i+1)-q_i)\n      =-d_i sigma_i <=0.                                   (7)\n\nFor a remote j>i+1, use y_i=y_(i+1)+g_(i+1)+d_i g_i. The fresh\ninnovation is orthogonal to g_j and g_(i+1), so\n\n    <y_i,g_j-g_(i+1)>\n      =<y_(i+1),g_j-g_(i+1)>\n       +(1+d_i t_i)<g_(i+1),g_j-g_(i+1)> <=0.                (8)\n\nThe first term is nonpositive by backward induction; the second is\nnonpositive by (5). Adding (7) proves the remote comparison. At i=n\nthere is no future vertex.\n\nFor a past j<i, write\n\n    g_j=(product_(k=j)^(i-1)t_k)g_i+w,\n    w in span{e_j,...,e_(i-1)}.\n\nSince y_i is orthogonal to w, the corresponding expression in (6) is\n(product t_k-1)delta_i<=0. This also establishes all the past\ncomparisons at the terminal index. Thus (6) includes every ordered\npair, however remote or in different recursive children.\n\nThe maximizer defining F is P_C(x), the Euclidean projection onto C.\nIndeed completing the square turns maximization into minimization of\n||v-x||^2. The projection characterization (6) gives P_C(x_i)=g_i.\nFor completeness the two projection inequalities at arbitrary x,z\ngive\n\n    <P_C(x)-P_C(z),x-z> >= ||P_C(x)-P_C(z)||^2.\n\nConsequently P_C is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing affine\nfunctions in the definition of F gives\n\n    <P_C(x),u> <= F(x+u)-F(x) <= <P_C(x+u),u>.\n\nThe difference from the leftmost linear expression is between zero\nand ||u||^2. Therefore F is differentiable, grad F=P_C, and its\ngradient is 1-Lipschitz. F is finite because C is compact, and convex\nbecause it is a supremum of affine functions of x.\n\nThe actual sampled values are\n\n    f_i=F(x_i)=delta_i+q_i/2.                                (9)\n\nInclude x_*=g_*=f_*=0 and y_*=0. For the original convention in\n1252b8823bb5a290, direct expansion gives the COMPLETE row identity\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<y_j,g_i-g_j> <=0.                                (10)\n\nIn particular Q_(i,*)=0 and Q_(*,j)=-delta_j<=0. No optimum row,\nincoming terminal row, or cross-block row was relaxed.\n\n## 4. Minimum set, true radius, and excess innovation price\n\nLet C^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in C}. Since 0 belongs to C,\nF>=0. If z is in C^polar, every maximand is at most zero, so F(z)=0.\nIf z is not in C^polar, choose a listed vertex g with <z,g>>0;\nat a sufficiently small positive multiple of g the maximand is\npositive. Thus\n\n    argmin F=C^polar,  min F=0.\n\nTelescoping the actual updates yields\n\n    x_0=eta g_n+sum_(i<n)h_i g_i,\n\na nonnegative combination of vertices. For every z in C^polar,\n<x_0,z><=0, and consequently\n\n    ||x_0-z||^2=||x_0||^2+||z||^2-2<x_0,z>>=||x_0||^2.\n\nEquality is attained at z=0. This proves the exact nearest-minimizer\nnormalization in Section 1, even though the minimum set need not be\na singleton. Equations (2),(3) price the whole dense initial anchor.\nAt the terminal point, (9) gives 2f_n=2eta-1.\n\nFor fixed eta,t, increasing any sigma_i can only increase r_0.\nTo see this without ignoring its influence on earlier gradients,\nobserve that the recurrences (1)--(3), as maps of the suffix state\n(r,delta,q), have nonnegative coefficients whenever 0<=t<=1 and d>0.\nAn increment in sigma adds respectively h^2 sigma, d sigma, sigma\nto the current state (r_i,delta_i,q_i). These nonnegative increments\nremain nonnegative at all earlier indices. Thus the best payoff in\nthe WHOLE declared family has sigma_i=0 for every i. This removes\narbitrary forward-adjacent normal slack by a paid monotonicity proof,\nnot by assuming that every optimal construction must saturate it.\n\nIsometric embeddings do not change the distances. A finite orthogonal\nsum of members of this family has a product minimizer set and payoff\nequal to the radius-squared-weighted mean of its component payoffs.\nIt therefore cannot exceed the family bound proved below.\n\n## 5. Exact reduction of all continuous correlations to binary choices\n\nSet sigma=0 for the rest of the optimization. At fixed eta the\nterminal state is (eta^2,eta-1,1). Each map (1)--(3) is linear in the\nsuffix state and affine in its own t_i. Each parameter occurs in only\none map. Their composition r_0(t_0,...,t_(n-1)) is thus affine\nseparately in every t_i. Repeatedly using\n\n    p(...,t_i,...)=(1-t_i)p(...,0,...)+t_i p(...,1,...)\n\ngives the exact convex-combination identity\n\n    r_0(t)=sum_(epsilon in {0,1}^n)\n       [product_(epsilon_i=1)t_i product_(epsilon_i=0)(1-t_i)]\n       r_0(epsilon).                                       (11)\n\nThe weights are nonnegative and sum to one. The numerator 2eta-1\nis the same for every term. Therefore\n\n    V(t,eta)<=max_(epsilon in {0,1}^n) V(epsilon,eta).         (12)\n\nThis is a mathematical vertex reduction, not an enumeration that was\nrun. It remains valid for horizon-dependent choices and after taking\nthe supremum over eta>=1. The fresh-vector square roots do not need\nto be affine: it is the exact radius state (1)--(3) that is affine.\n\nFor a binary choice, t_i=1 repeats the same gradient. At t_i=0 the\ncurrent gradient is orthogonal to EVERY later gradient, with norm\nq_i=(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))/d_i. Thus the binary choices partition the\nword into constant-gradient phases separated by selected physical\ncheckpoint steps. Repeated physical queries remain in the word;\nonly the algebra for a phase is compressed.\n\nWrite the exact partition as\n\n    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k),\n\nwhere s_j is the mass of block_j and d_j^cp=b_j-1>0. The superscript\ncp distinguishes checkpoint offsets from the physical indexed d_i.\nDefine a paid scalar A by starting at A=0 and, for each checkpoint,\nupdating\n\n    T=s_j+b_j,    A <- T(T+A)/d_j^cp.                        (13)\n\nAt the end let r=s_k and X=r+eta. The exact squared radius is\n\n    r_0=X(X+A),   V=(2X-D)/[X(X+A)],  D=2r+1.                (14)\n\nTo verify (13), denote the mutually orthogonal phase gradients by\np_j and their squared norms by Q_j^phase. Put T_j=s_j+b_j for j<k\nand T_k=X=s_k+eta. At the checkpoint ending phase j, its current\ncoordinate is b_j p_j and is extinguished by that step. Hence the\ninitial point is exactly sum_(j=0)^k T_j p_j. The norm recursion\nin (1), evaluated at each checkpoint, is\n\n    Q_j^phase=(T_(j+1)/d_j^cp)Q_(j+1)^phase,\n    Q_k^phase=1.\n\nFor a nonterminal next phase this uses\ndelta_next+q_next=(s_(j+1)+b_(j+1))Q_(j+1)^phase;\nfor the final phase it uses delta_next+q_next=X. Define\n\n    A_j=[sum_(ell<j)T_ell^2 Q_ell^phase]/[T_j Q_j^phase].\n\nThen A_0=0 and\nA_(j+1)=T_j(T_j+A_j)/d_j^cp by substitution of the norm ratio.\nThe complete squared radius is\nsum_(j=0)^k T_j^2 Q_j^phase=X^2+A_k X, proving (13),(14).\nFor k=0, A=0 and X=sum h+eta, so the same formula applies.\n\nThe maximum over eta>=1 is attained at\n\n    X_*=(D+sqrt(D^2+2AD))/2,\n    V_* = 2/[A+D+sqrt(D^2+2AD)].                             (15)\n\nIndeed differentiating (14) gives a numerator\n-2X^2+2DX+AD, positive before X_* and negative after it.\nAlso X_*>=D>=r+1, so the maximizer lies in the permitted domain.\n\n## 6. A local checkpoint-removal lemma with both boundary cases paid\n\nConsider a checkpoint b=1+d with ordinary mass s immediately before\nit, and put T=s+b. Let A>=0 be the accumulated paid state BEFORE this\nphase. Including this checkpoint changes it to\n\n    A_b=T(T+A)/d.\n\nIf a later checkpoint c exists, let r be the ordinary mass between\nb and c, and X=r+c. At c, including b produces the numerator\nX(X+A_b) in update (13); deleting b produces\n(T+X)(T+X+A). Their exact difference is\n\n    Delta = X[A_b-2T-A]-T(T+A)\n          = T[X(T-2d)/d-T]\n             +A[X(T-d)/d-T].                              (16)\n\nIf b is the last checkpoint, precisely the same difference is the\ndifference of the two final radii, now with X=r+eta.\n\nIf T>2d and\n\n    X >= dT/(T-2d),                                       (17)\n\nboth brackets in (16) are nonnegative. The condition for the second\nbracket is weaker because T-d>T-2d. Deleting b then cannot increase\nthe radius at that same eta. If c exists, its denominator c-1 is\nunchanged, and every later update (13) is increasing in its input\nA; thus this local comparison propagates to the actual terminal.\n\nThere is a separate last-checkpoint argument. Suppose\n\n    s+1>=2d,   r+1>=2d.                                   (18)\n\nThen T>=3d and D=2r+1>=2d. At the optimizer of the split word,\nformula (15), with A_b in place of A, gives\n\n    X_*>=sqrt(A_b D/2)>=sqrt(T^2 D/(2d))>=T.\n\nSince T>=3d, one has T>=dT/(T-2d). Thus (17) holds at the\nSPLIT word's optimizer. The merged word at this same eta has at\nleast the split word's optimized payoff. Its own optimized payoff\ncan only be larger. This proves the required comparison even though\ndeleting the last checkpoint need not improve radius at every eta.\n\n## 7. Applying the removal lemma to EVERY checkpoint subset on H_m\n\nFix m>=32, and suppress the subscript m on a,c,beta. All children\nretain these same parameters. Write S_j=sum H_j and M_j=1+S_j.\nThen\n\n    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j,\n    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2).                                       (19)\n\nHere 0<A_mass<1. Indeed c>lambda>2, c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1,\nwhereas ac=beta<1. Moreover sqrt(2)<99/70, because\n99^2>2*70^2. Therefore\n\n    c <= (169/70)(33/32)=5577/2240 < 5/2.\n\nFor a physical step of ruler level ell, its offset is d=a c^ell.\nEquation (19) gives\n\n    d/M_ell <= c-2 < 1/2.                                (20)\n\nNow take ANY nonempty selected checkpoint set, not necessarily a\ndyadic partition. Choose a checkpoint of smallest ruler level ell\namong those selected. Each of its two adjoining actual H_ell\nchildren contains no selected checkpoint, since every level in such\na child is smaller than ell. Hence the ordinary blocks immediately\nbefore and after it both have mass at least S_ell. Put M=M_ell.\n\nIf a later selected checkpoint c_cp exists, its level is at least ell,\nso its offset is at least d. With the notation of Section 6,\n\n    T>=M+d,   X>=M+d,   d<M/2.\n\nThe function T -> dT/(T-2d) decreases for T>2d. Thus\n\n    dT/(T-2d) <= d(M+d)/(M-d) <= M+d <= X.\n\nThe deletion is legitimate by (16),(17). No other selected step\nor physical ordinary step is altered.\n\nIf the chosen checkpoint is last, the same adjoining-child property\ngives s+1>=M>2d and r+1>=M>2d. Conditions (18) apply. Hence its\ndeletion cannot decrease the optimized payoff either.\n\nAfter deletion, choose a smallest-level checkpoint in the remaining\nset and repeat the SAME argument. It applies to arbitrary subsets,\nso it does not assume that the new set is dyadic. Finitely many\ndeletions end with no checkpoints. Formula (14) then becomes\n\n    V=(2eta-1)/(S_m+eta)^2.\n\nIts maximum over eta>=1 is at eta=S_m+1 and equals\n1/(2S_m+1). Combining this with (12) and the monotonicity in sigma\nproves the upper bound in (T) for every original parameter choice.\nThe all-t=1, all-sigma=0 member attains it. This proves equality.\n\n## 8. Same-word references and the exact original benchmark price\n\nSet Huber_m=1/(2S_m+1). Accepted b98bb807d514e80d and\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a, with their COMPLETE checkpoint definitions, give\n\n    sup V=Huber_m <= K_n(H_m) <= F_ray_n(H_m).\n\nThis comparison does not select one ray checkpoint or ignore any\nfeasible multi-cut choice. It does not require deciding whether\neither reference is strictly larger than Huber on this word.\n\nFor clarity let C_full(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z), using EVERY original\ninterpolation cycle in D_h from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf, and the\naccepted specified initial point 1 and specified minimizer 0.\nThen\n\n    sup V <= max{Huber_m,C_full(H_m)}.\n\nThere is also an explicit comparison to the UNaugmented full complex\nchannel: for every nonnegative word of mass S, set theta=1/(2S+1)\nand P=product_(i<n)(1-h_i theta)>0. Then\n\n    Gap_h(theta)=(2theta-theta^2)P^2 >= theta/2.              (21)\n\nHere theta lies in the FULL D_h because its imaginary part is zero\nand theta-theta^2>=0. To prove equality, all queried scalar points\nPoly_i(theta) lie in [P,1]. Define a continuous nondecreasing\n1-Lipschitz derivative on the real line by\n\n    F'(x)=x                         for x<=theta P,\n    F'(x)=theta P                   for theta P<=x<=P,\n    F'(x)=theta x                   for x>=P,\n    F(0)=0.\n\nThe matching values at the two joins make this derivative well\ndefined; its slopes are 1,0,theta. Its unique zero is 0. Integration\ngives F(P)=(theta-theta^2/2)P^2, while at each queried positive point\nits derivative is theta Poly_i(theta). This is the required one\nstatic scalar realization. The accepted upper bound on Gap_h at\nreal theta is the same number, proving equality. Finally,\nproduct(1-h_i theta)>=1-S theta=(1+theta)/2: prove this by induction\nusing nonnegative factors and partial sums at most S theta<1.\nThus Gap_h(theta)>=theta(2-theta)(1+theta)^2/4>=theta/2,\nas (2-theta)(1+theta)^2=2+3theta-theta^3>=2.\n\nConsequently the exact family supremum satisfies\n\n    (sup V)/C_full(H_m) <= 2.\n\nNo assertion C_full>=Huber is made. The full complex supremum is\nNOT replaced by the universal lens or by the single parameter in\n(21); (21) is only a valid lower bound within that full channel.\nThe family already loses to the combined accepted references\nbecause it cannot exceed their Huber component.\n\nFor the ORIGINAL benchmark define C_0^bench=B_0=1 and\n\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n^bench=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k^bench,C_(n-k-1)^bench),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k^bench,B_(n-k-1)).\n\nThese are exactly the accepted recurrences, not a balanced-tree\nsurrogate. Equations (T),(19) give the exact normalized family ratio\n\n    B_(2^m-1) sup V\n      = B_(2^m-1)/[2 A_mass c_m^m+2(1-A_mass)2^m-1].        (22)\n\nThe unconditional assertion in accepted 04219554e41d9750 supplies\n\n    Z_1=lim_(m->infinity)lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1),\n    0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2,\n\nwhere B_1=G(1,1)=4. Huber dominance here would\nbe false; the small-ruler-offset condition (20) does real work.\n\nSecond, deletion at a FIXED terminal eta is false even on the\noriginal H_m. (24)\n\nFor m>=32, q>sqrt(2)-1, so (24) is negative. Here is a direct\nenclosing-m check rather than an illicit critical-parameter substitution.\nAt level 2,\n\n    d_2=lambda(1-1/m^2),\n    M_2=lambda^2-1/m-4/[lambda(m+1)].\n\nThe inequality lambda d_2>M_2 is equivalent to\nm+4m^2/[lambda(m+1)]>lambda^2, which follows from m>=32 and\nlambda^2<25/4. Moreover\n(d_(ell+1)/M_(ell+1))=c(d_ell/M_ell)/(2+d_ell/M_ell),\nand d_ell/M_ell<c-2, so these ratios increase with ell. Thus the\ntop-level ratio is at least the level-2 ratio. Since\n1/q-2-q<0 exactly when q>sqrt(2)-1, the counterexample follows.\nIt does not contradict (T), which optimizes the terminal ratio.\n\nThird, nonnegative backward coefficients larger than one do not\nsilently reopen this same geometry. In any legal realization with\nthe same fresh-innovation orthogonality and radial terminal point,\nthe reverse adjacent normal gives\n\n    <y_(i+1),g_i-g_(i+1)>=(t_i-1)delta_(i+1)<=0.             (25)\n\nFor eta>1 every reserve is positive, forcing t_i<=1. For eta=1,\nall reserves before the terminal one are still positive: from\ndelta_i=t_i(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))+d_i q_i, they are positive when\nt_i>=0 and q_i>0. In that case\nq_(n-1)>=t_(n-1)^2 by the orthogonal decomposition. Replacing this\nlast extension by t=1,q=1 reduces each of q_(n-1),delta_(n-1),\nr_(n-1). Keep all earlier t in [0,1] and their nonnegative sigma\nas derived from their original forward adjacent inequalities.\nThe nonnegative state maps then reduce the initial radius as well.\nThe replacement is a member of (1). This is\nan optional enlargement of the obstruction, not an assumption used\nto construct the pilot.\n\nThe closure DOES NOT cover negative backward coefficients; a new\ninnovation correlated with a second future mode; arbitrary conjugate\nheights in place of the projection-value form (9); or an independently\nmoving transverse terminal position. Returning\nthis manuscript is not evidence that the outer loop has already exited.\n\nThe original all-word, all-sufficiently-large-horizon Goal remains OPEN.\nThe normalization throughout is W=2R: at smoothness one the payoff is\ntwice the final objective gap divided by the squared distance from the\ninitial point to the ENTIRE minimizer set.\n\nAccepted inputs are 1252b8823bb5a290 for the original interpolation\nconvention, b98bb807d514e80d for K and the original benchmark,\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a for the COMPLETE alternating-ray reference,\ndfde2fa326c908bf for the FULL schedule-dependent complex reference,\nand the UNCONDITIONAL denominator-ray assertion of 04219554e41d9750.\nNo conditional seed existence is assumed. No earlier unverified family\nclosure is a mathematical premise. All new ingredients below are proved\ntogether, rather than promoted to facts and reused without a gate.\n\n## 1. New state, explicit data, and result\n\nFor any physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) with every h_i>1, set\nd_i=h_i-1. Choose eta>=1, tail coefficients t_i in [0,1], and innovation\nslacks sigma_i>=0. These are explicit scalar inputs, not a Gram or\nfunction-value feasibility oracle. Use the orthonormal basis\ne_0,...,e_n of R^(n+1). Starting at\n\n    g_n=e_n,  x_n=eta e_n,  q_n=1,  delta_n=eta-1,\n\nconstruct backward, for i=n-1,...,0, by\n\n    q_i=t_i q_(i+1)+(1-t_i)(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))/d_i+sigma_i,\n    v_i^2=q_i-t_i^2 q_(i+1),\n    g_i=t_i g_(i+1)+v_i e_i,\n    x_i=x_(i+1)+h_i g_i.                                      (1)\n\nHere v_i is the NONNEGATIVE square root. The reserve delta_i will be\ncomputed by (2) below; it is <x_i,g_i>-q_i. Thus every scalar in (1)\nis obtained by a finite specified recursion before the next index is\nconstructed. No sample is discarded or re-indexed.\n\nDefine the one static function for this full run by\n\n    C=conv{0,g_0,...,g_n},\n    F(x)=max_(v in C) {<x,v>-||v||^2/2}.\n\nThe new dependence is an overlapping future gradient plus a fresh\northogonal innovation at each physical step. For example,\nt_i=beta_m and sigma_i=0 at every i gives a genuinely changing,\npositively correlated law on the original H_m below. Its dimension\nand number of listed gradient vertices grow with m. Every coordinate\nthat survives several steps can change amplitude at all those steps.\nThis is not a common added gradient or an orthogonal sum of old\ntriangle constructions.\n\nThis draft proves that ALL choices in (1) are legal, with exact payoff\n\n    V=(2eta-1)/Rtail_0_sq,  Rtail_0_sq=||x_0||^2\n      =dist(x_0,argmin F)^2.\n\nNevertheless, for the ORIGINAL enclosing-m parameters\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,\n    c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1,\n\nthe entire declared family has, for every m>=32, exactly\n\n    sup_(eta,t,sigma) V = 1/(1+2 sum H_m).                     (T)\n\nIn particular this new positive-correlation law cannot improve on\nHuber, the complete same-word K, or the complete same-word ray channel.\nThe exact original-B normalization and full-complex comparison are in\nSection 8. The limiting family/B ratio is strictly below one.\n\nThe law leaves the nonpositive-adjacent-correlation hypotheses of\nC98/C100. It also permits short-step reserves larger than the C99 cap:\nalready the all-t=1 choice has delta_i/q_i equal to a whole remaining\nmass plus eta-1. Thus the new closure is not obtained by renaming a\npreviously excluded negative-correlation or capped-reserve law.\n\n## 2. Scalar identities and actual positive semidefinite geometry\n\nSuppose the suffix has been constructed. Its points and gradients lie\nin span{e_(i+1),...,e_n}. Put q=q_(i+1), delta=delta_(i+1), t=t_i,\nd=d_i, h=1+d, and sigma=sigma_i. Then\n\n    v_i^2=(1-t)[t q+(delta+q)/d]+sigma >=0.\n\nThe vectors in (1), not merely their pairwise proposed correlations,\ntherefore give a real positive semidefinite Gram matrix. Taking inner\nproducts in x_i=x_(i+1)+h g_i gives\n\n    delta_i = delta+(1+d t)q+d sigma,                         (2)\n    Rtail_i_sq = Rtail_(i+1)_sq+h^2 q_i+2h t(delta+q),                     (3)\n\nHere Rtail_i_sq=||x_i||^2; R_sq denotes a generic squared-radius state.\nThe fresh Rtail notation avoids the accepted ray channel's different r_i.\n These identities also prove delta_i>=0 and q_i>0\nat every index. All dimensions are physical dimensions, not radii.\n\nFor i<j, orthogonality of the innovations gives\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=(product_(k=i)^(j-1) t_k) q_j >=0.              (4)\n\nAlso q_i>=t_i q_(i+1). Repeated use of this inequality implies\n\n    <g_i,g_j><=q_i  for every i<j.                            (5)\n\nFor the concrete all-t=beta_m pilot, 0<t_i<1 and v_i>0 at every\nphysical index. Its gradients are all distinct, and adjacent inner\nproducts are strictly positive. This statement follows symbolically\nfrom (1), without evaluating any instance.\n\n## 3. Every original ordered row, including the remote rows\n\nLet y_i=x_i-g_i. We prove that\n\n    <y_i,g_j-g_i><=0 for every i,j in {0,...,n},\n    <y_i,-g_i><=0.                                          (6)\n\nThe second inequality is -delta_i<=0. The adjacent forward comparison\nhas the exact value\n\n    <y_i,g_(i+1)-g_i>\n      =(1-t_i)(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))\n        +d_i(t_i q_(i+1)-q_i)\n      =-d_i sigma_i <=0.                                   (7)\n\nFor a remote j>i+1, use y_i=y_(i+1)+g_(i+1)+d_i g_i. The fresh\ninnovation is orthogonal to g_j and g_(i+1), so\n\n    <y_i,g_j-g_(i+1)>\n      =<y_(i+1),g_j-g_(i+1)>\n       +(1+d_i t_i)<g_(i+1),g_j-g_(i+1)> <=0.                (8)\n\nThe first term is nonpositive by backward induction; the second is\nnonpositive by (5). Adding (7) proves the remote comparison. At i=n\nthere is no future vertex.\n\nFor a past j<i, write\n\n    g_j=(product_(k=j)^(i-1)t_k)g_i+w,\n    w in span{e_j,...,e_(i-1)}.\n\nSince y_i is orthogonal to w, the corresponding expression in (6) is\n(product t_k-1)delta_i<=0. This also establishes all the past\ncomparisons at the terminal index. Thus (6) includes every ordered\npair, however remote or in different recursive children.\n\nThe maximizer defining F is P_C(x), the Euclidean projection onto C.\nIndeed completing the square turns maximization into minimization of\n||v-x||^2. The projection characterization (6) gives P_C(x_i)=g_i.\nFor completeness the two projection inequalities at arbitrary x,z\ngive\n\n    <P_C(x)-P_C(z),x-z> >= ||P_C(x)-P_C(z)||^2.\n\nConsequently P_C is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing affine\nfunctions in the definition of F gives\n\n    <P_C(x),u> <= F(x+u)-F(x) <= <P_C(x+u),u>.\n\nThe difference from the leftmost linear expression is between zero\nand ||u||^2. Therefore F is differentiable, grad F=P_C, and its\ngradient is 1-Lipschitz. F is finite because C is compact, and convex\nbecause it is a supremum of affine functions of x.\n\nThe actual sampled values are\n\n    f_i=F(x_i)=delta_i+q_i/2.                                (9)\n\nInclude x_*=g_*=f_*=0 and y_*=0. For the original convention in\n1252b8823bb5a290, direct expansion gives the COMPLETE row identity\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<y_j,g_i-g_j> <=0.                                (10)\n\nIn particular Q_(i,*)=0 and Q_(*,j)=-delta_j<=0. No optimum row,\nincoming terminal row, or cross-block row was relaxed.\n\n## 4. Minimum set, true radius, and excess innovation price\n\nLet C^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in C}. Since 0 belongs to C,\nF>=0. If z is in C^polar, every maximand is at most zero, so F(z)=0.\nIf z is not in C^polar, choose a listed vertex g with <z,g>>0;\nat a sufficiently small positive multiple of g the maximand is\npositive. Thus\n\n    argmin F=C^polar,  min F=0.\n\nTelescoping the actual updates yields\n\n    x_0=eta g_n+sum_(i<n)h_i g_i,\n\na nonnegative combination of vertices. For every z in C^polar,\n<x_0,z><=0, and consequently\n\n    ||x_0-z||^2=||x_0||^2+||z||^2-2<x_0,z>>=||x_0||^2.\n\nEquality is attained at z=0. This proves the exact nearest-minimizer\nnormalization in Section 1, even though the minimum set need not be\na singleton. Equations (2),(3) price the whole dense initial anchor.\nAt the terminal point, (9) gives 2f_n=2eta-1. Multiplying F by any\nL>0 and using the physical steps h_i/L preserves all points and this\nnormalized payoff.\n\nFor fixed eta,t, increasing any sigma_i can only increase Rtail_0_sq.\nTo see this without ignoring its influence on earlier gradients,\nobserve that the recurrences (1)--(3), as maps of the suffix state\n(R_sq,delta,q), have nonnegative coefficients whenever 0<=t<=1 and d>0.\nAn increment in sigma adds respectively h^2 sigma, d sigma, sigma\nto the current state (Rtail_i_sq,delta_i,q_i). These nonnegative increments\nremain nonnegative at all earlier indices. Thus the best payoff in\nthe WHOLE declared family has sigma_i=0 for every i. This removes\narbitrary forward-adjacent normal slack by a paid monotonicity proof,\nnot by assuming that every optimal construction must saturate it.\n\nIsometric embeddings do not change the distances. A finite orthogonal\nsum of members of this family has a product minimizer set and payoff\nequal to the radius-squared-weighted mean of its component payoffs.\nIt therefore cannot exceed the family bound proved below.\n\n## 5. Exact reduction of all continuous correlations to binary choices\n\nSet sigma=0 for the rest of the optimization. At fixed eta the\nterminal state is (eta^2,eta-1,1). Each map (1)--(3) is linear in the\nsuffix state and affine in its own t_i. Each parameter occurs in only\none map. Their composition Rtail_0_sq(t_0,...,t_(n-1)) is thus affine\nseparately in every t_i. Repeatedly using\n\n    p(...,t_i,...)=(1-t_i)p(...,0,...)+t_i p(...,1,...)\n\ngives the exact convex-combination identity\n\n    Rtail_0_sq(t)=sum_(epsilon in {0,1}^n)\n       [product_(epsilon_i=1)t_i product_(epsilon_i=0)(1-t_i)]\n       Rtail_0_sq(epsilon).                                       (11)\n\nThe weights are nonnegative and sum to one. The numerator 2eta-1\nis the same for every term. Therefore\n\n    V(t,eta)<=max_(epsilon in {0,1}^n) V(epsilon,eta).         (12)\n\nThis is a mathematical vertex reduction, not an enumeration that was\nrun. It remains valid for horizon-dependent choices and after taking\nthe supremum over eta>=1. The fresh-vector square roots do not need\nto be affine: it is the exact radius state (1)--(3) that is affine.\n\nFor a binary choice, t_i=1 repeats the same gradient. At t_i=0 the\ncurrent gradient is orthogonal to EVERY later gradient, with norm\nq_i=(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))/d_i. Thus the binary choices partition the\nword into constant-gradient phases separated by selected physical\ncheckpoint steps. Repeated physical queries remain in the word;\nonly the algebra for a phase is compressed.\n\nWrite the exact partition as\n\n    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k),\n\nwhere s_j is the mass of block_j and d_j^cp=b_j-1>0. The superscript\ncp distinguishes checkpoint offsets from the physical indexed d_i.\nDefine a paid scalar A by starting at A=0 and, for each checkpoint,\nupdating\n\n    T=s_j+b_j,    A <- T(T+A)/d_j^cp.                        (13)\n\nAt the end let r=s_k and X=r+eta. The exact squared radius is\n\n    Rtail_0_sq=X(X+A),   V=(2X-D)/[X(X+A)],  D=2r+1.                (14)\n\nTo verify (13), denote the mutually orthogonal phase gradients by\np_j and their squared norms by Q_j^phase. Put T_j=s_j+b_j for j<k\nand T_k=X=s_k+eta. At the checkpoint ending phase j, its current\ncoordinate is b_j p_j and is extinguished by that step. Hence the\ninitial point is exactly sum_(j=0)^k T_j p_j. The norm recursion\nin (1), evaluated at each checkpoint, is\n\n    Q_j^phase=(T_(j+1)/d_j^cp)Q_(j+1)^phase,\n    Q_k^phase=1.\n\nFor a nonterminal next phase this uses\ndelta_next+q_next=(s_(j+1)+b_(j+1))Q_(j+1)^phase;\nfor the final phase it uses delta_next+q_next=X. Define\n\n    A_j=[sum_(ell<j)T_ell^2 Q_ell^phase]/[T_j Q_j^phase].\n\nThen A_0=0 and\nA_(j+1)=T_j(T_j+A_j)/d_j^cp by substitution of the norm ratio.\nThe complete squared radius is\nsum_(j=0)^k T_j^2 Q_j^phase=X^2+A_k X, proving (13),(14).\nFor k=0, A=0 and X=sum h+eta, so the same formula applies.\n\nThe maximum over eta>=1 is attained at\n\n    X_*=(D+sqrt(D^2+2AD))/2,\n    V_* = 2/[A+D+sqrt(D^2+2AD)].                             (15)\n\nIndeed differentiating (14) gives a numerator\n-2X^2+2DX+AD, positive before X_* and negative after it.\nAlso X_*>=D>=r+1, so the maximizer lies in the permitted domain.\n\n## 6. A local checkpoint-removal lemma with both boundary cases paid\n\nConsider a checkpoint b=1+d with ordinary mass s immediately before\nit, and put T=s+b. Let A>=0 be the accumulated paid state BEFORE this\nphase. Including this checkpoint changes it to\n\n    A_b=T(T+A)/d.\n\nIf a later checkpoint c exists, let r be the ordinary mass between\nb and c, and X=r+c. At c, including b produces the numerator\nX(X+A_b) in update (13); deleting b produces\n(T+X)(T+X+A). Their exact difference is\n\n    Delta = X[A_b-2T-A]-T(T+A)\n          = T[X(T-2d)/d-T]\n             +A[X(T-d)/d-T].                              (16)\n\nIf b is the last checkpoint, precisely the same difference is the\ndifference of the two final radii, now with X=r+eta.\n\nIf T>2d and\n\n    X >= dT/(T-2d),                                       (17)\n\nboth brackets in (16) are nonnegative. The condition for the second\nbracket is weaker because T-d>T-2d. Deleting b then cannot increase\nthe radius at that same eta. If c exists, its denominator c-1 is\nunchanged, and every later update (13) is increasing in its input\nA; thus this local comparison propagates to the actual terminal.\n\nThere is a separate last-checkpoint argument. Suppose\n\n    s+1>=2d,   r+1>=2d.                                   (18)\n\nThen T>=3d and D=2r+1>=2d. At the optimizer of the split word,\nformula (15), with A_b in place of A, gives\n\n    X_*>=sqrt(A_b D/2)>=sqrt(T^2 D/(2d))>=T.\n\nSince T>=3d, one has T>=dT/(T-2d). Thus (17) holds at the\nSPLIT word's optimizer. The merged word at this same eta has at\nleast the split word's optimized payoff. Its own optimized payoff\ncan only be larger. This proves the required comparison even though\ndeleting the last checkpoint need not improve radius at every eta.\n\n## 7. Applying the removal lemma to EVERY checkpoint subset on H_m\n\nFix m>=32, and suppress the subscript m on a,c,beta. All children\nretain these same parameters. Write S_j=sum H_j and M_j=1+S_j.\nThen\n\n    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j,\n    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2).                                       (19)\n\nHere 0<A_mass<1. Indeed c>lambda>2, c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1,\nwhereas ac=beta<1. Moreover sqrt(2)<99/70, because\n99^2>2*70^2. Therefore\n\n    c <= (169/70)(33/32)=5577/2240 < 5/2.\n\nFor a physical step of ruler level ell, its offset is d=a c^ell.\nEquation (19) gives\n\n    d/M_ell <= c-2 < 1/2.                                (20)\n\nNow take ANY nonempty selected checkpoint set, not necessarily a\ndyadic partition. Choose a checkpoint of smallest ruler level ell\namong those selected. Each of its two adjoining actual H_ell\nchildren contains no selected checkpoint, since every level in such\na child is smaller than ell. Hence the ordinary blocks immediately\nbefore and after it both have mass at least S_ell. Put M=M_ell.\n\nIf a later selected checkpoint c_cp exists, its level is at least ell,\nso its offset is at least d. With the notation of Section 6,\n\n    T>=M+d,   X>=M+d,   d<M/2.\n\nThe function T -> dT/(T-2d) decreases for T>2d. Thus\n\n    dT/(T-2d) <= d(M+d)/(M-d) <= M+d <= X.\n\nThe deletion is legitimate by (16),(17). No other selected step\nor physical ordinary step is altered.\n\nIf the chosen checkpoint is last, the same adjoining-child property\ngives s+1>=M>2d and r+1>=M>2d. Conditions (18) apply. Hence its\ndeletion cannot decrease the optimized payoff either.\n\nAfter deletion, choose a smallest-level checkpoint in the remaining\nset and repeat the SAME argument. It applies to arbitrary subsets,\nso it does not assume that the new set is dyadic. Finitely many\ndeletions end with no checkpoints. Formula (14) then becomes\n\n    V=(2eta-1)/(S_m+eta)^2.\n\nIts maximum over eta>=1 is at eta=S_m+1 and equals\n1/(2S_m+1). Combining this with (12) and the monotonicity in sigma\nproves the upper bound in (T) for every original parameter choice.\nThe all-t=1, all-sigma=0 member attains it. This proves equality.\n\n## 8. Same-word references and the exact original benchmark price\n\nSet Huber_m=1/(2S_m+1). Accepted b98bb807d514e80d and\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a, with their COMPLETE checkpoint definitions, give\n\n    sup V=Huber_m <= K_n(H_m) <= F_ray_n(H_m).\n\nThis comparison does not select one ray checkpoint or ignore any\nfeasible multi-cut choice. It does not require deciding whether\neither reference is strictly larger than Huber on this word.\n\nFor clarity let C_full(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z), using EVERY original\ninterpolation cycle in D_h from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf, and the\naccepted specified initial point 1 and specified minimizer 0.\nThen\n\n    sup V <= max{Huber_m,C_full(H_m)}.\n\nThere is also an explicit comparison to the UNaugmented full complex\nchannel: for every nonnegative word of mass S, set theta=1/(2S+1)\nand P=product_(i<n)(1-h_i theta)>0. Then\n\n    Gap_h(theta)=(2theta-theta^2)P^2 >= theta/2.              (21)\n\nHere theta lies in the FULL D_h because its imaginary part is zero\nand theta-theta^2>=0. To prove equality, all queried scalar points\nPoly_i(theta) lie in [P,1]. Define a continuous nondecreasing\n1-Lipschitz derivative on the real line by\n\n    F'(x)=x                         for x<=theta P,\n    F'(x)=theta P                   for theta P<=x<=P,\n    F'(x)=theta x                   for x>=P,\n    F(0)=0.\n\nThe matching values at the two joins make this derivative well\ndefined; its slopes are 1,0,theta. Its unique zero is 0. Integration\ngives F(P)=(theta-theta^2/2)P^2, while at each queried positive point\nits derivative is theta Poly_i(theta). This is the required one\nstatic scalar realization. The accepted upper bound on Gap_h at\nreal theta is the same number, proving equality. Finally,\nproduct(1-h_i theta)>=1-S theta=(1+theta)/2: prove this by induction\nusing nonnegative factors and partial sums at most S theta<1.\nThus Gap_h(theta)>=theta(2-theta)(1+theta)^2/4>=theta/2,\nas (2-theta)(1+theta)^2=2+3theta-theta^3>=2.\n\nConsequently the exact family supremum satisfies\n\n    (sup V)/C_full(H_m) <= 2.\n\nNo assertion C_full>=Huber is made. The full complex supremum is\nNOT replaced by the universal lens or by the single parameter in\n(21); (21) is only a valid lower bound within that full channel.\nThe family already loses to the combined accepted references\nbecause it cannot exceed their Huber component.\n\nFor the ORIGINAL benchmark define C_0^bench=B_0=1 and\n\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n^bench=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k^bench,C_(n-k-1)^bench),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k^bench,B_(n-k-1)).\n\nThese are exactly the accepted recurrences, not a balanced-tree\nsurrogate. Equations (T),(19) give the exact normalized family ratio\n\n    B_(2^m-1) sup V\n      = B_(2^m-1)/[2 A_mass c_m^m+2(1-A_mass)2^m-1].        (22)\n\nThe unconditional assertion in accepted 04219554e41d9750 supplies\n\n    Z_1=lim_(m->infinity)lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1),\n    0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2,\n\nwhere B_1=G(1,1)=4. Meanwhile A_mass tends to one and\n\n    c_m^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m -> exp(1),\n    (1-A_mass)(2/lambda)^m ->0.\n\nTherefore the exact limiting price is\n\n    lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V = Z_1/[2 exp(1)] < 1.  (23)\n\nFor a rational strict separation, exp(1)>5/2 and\n(203/100)^2<5, so this limit is strictly below 41209/50000.\nThis is a fixed relative shortfall of the NEW declared family,\nnot a certified full-class upper improvement for unrestricted W.\n\n## 9. Adversarial checks and the exact boundary of the conclusion\n\nFirst, (T) is not a theorem for all step words. On h=(10), choose\neta=2, t_0=0, sigma_0=0. Then\n\n    g_1=e_1, x_1=2e_1, g_0=(sqrt(2)/3)e_0,\n    x_0=(10sqrt(2)/3)e_0+2e_1,\n    Rtail_0_sq=236/9, V=27/236 > 1/21.\n\nAll data belong to the same explicit family, and its static realization,\nall ordered rows and true minimum distance were proved in Sections\n2--4. The last inequality is 27*21>236. Huber dominance here would\nbe false; the small-ruler-offset condition (20) does real work.\n\nSecond, deletion at a FIXED terminal eta is false even on the\noriginal H_m. This guards the optimizer step in Section 6. Use only\nthe central highest-level checkpoint, and put\n\n    M=M_(m-1), d=a_m c_m^(m-1), q=d/M,\n    s=r=M-1, T=M+d, eta=1, A=0.\n\nThen (16) with X=M gives\n\n    Rtail_split_sq-Rtail_merged_sq = T M(1/q-2-q).                        (24)\n\nFor m>=32, q>sqrt(2)-1, so (24) is negative. Here is a direct\nenclosing-m check rather than an illicit critical-parameter substitution.\nAt level 2,\n\n    d_2=lambda(1-1/m^2),\n    M_2=lambda^2-1/m-4/[lambda(m+1)].\n\nThe inequality lambda d_2>M_2 is equivalent to\nm+4m^2/[lambda(m+1)]>lambda^2, which follows from m>=32 and\nlambda^2<25/4. Moreover\n(d_(ell+1)/M_(ell+1))=c(d_ell/M_ell)/(2+d_ell/M_ell),\nand d_ell/M_ell<c-2, so these ratios increase with ell. Thus the\ntop-level ratio is at least the level-2 ratio. Since\n1/q-2-q<0 exactly when q>sqrt(2)-1, the counterexample follows.\nIt does not contradict (T), which optimizes the terminal ratio.\n\nThird, nonnegative backward coefficients larger than one do not\nsilently reopen this same geometry. In any legal realization with\nthe same fresh-innovation orthogonality and radial terminal point,\nthe reverse adjacent normal gives\n\n    <y_(i+1),g_i-g_(i+1)>=(t_i-1)delta_(i+1)<=0.             (25)\n\nFor eta>1 every reserve is positive, forcing t_i<=1. For eta=1,\nall reserves before the terminal one are still positive: from\ndelta_i=t_i(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))+d_i q_i, they are positive when\nt_i>=0 and q_i>0. A legal nonzero-terminal run cannot have q_i=0,\nsince it would reach a minimizer and then remain there. Thus only\nt_(n-1)>1 is possible, and only when eta=1. In that case\nq_(n-1)>=t_(n-1)^2 by the orthogonal decomposition. Replacing this\nlast extension by t=1,q=1 reduces each of q_(n-1),delta_(n-1),\nRtail_(n-1)_sq. Keep all earlier t in [0,1] and their nonnegative sigma\nas derived from their original forward adjacent inequalities.\nThe nonnegative state maps then reduce the initial radius as well.\nThe replacement is a member of (1). Therefore the same upper bound\nalso covers this exceptional nonnegative coefficient case. This is\nan optional enlargement of the obstruction, not an assumption used\nto construct the pilot.\n\nThe closure DOES NOT cover negative backward coefficients; a new\ninnovation correlated with a second future mode; arbitrary conjugate\nheights in place of the projection-value form (9); or an independently\nmoving transverse terminal position. It is not a closure of all\npositively correlated gradients, all Markov laws, or all smooth\ninterpolants. It does not reinterpret the accepted finite H6 witness\nas an asymptotic object.\n\n## 10. Literature, proof state, and remaining allocation\n\nMatlas searches were performed for smooth projection-conjugate\nrealizations and for the separate-affinity vertex principle. The\nfirst returned the generic Moreau-envelope Lemma 4.1 of Khanh,\nMordukhovich and Tran, arXiv:2303.08785. It supplies no special\ncorrelation law or quantitative radius bound here, so it is not\nimported. The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper\narXiv:1502.05666 was reread at Sections 2.4--2.5, Theorem 4's proof\nand Corollary 1. Its interpolation construction motivates the\nnormal-coordinate audit; all needed smoothness and pair claims\nare nevertheless derived directly in Section 3, using the accepted\nproject convention.\n\nMatlas identified Ben Sassi and Girard, \"Computation of Polytopic\nInvariants for Polynomial Dynamical Systems using Linear Programming,\"\narXiv:1012.1256, Lemma 2.2. Its statement is: a multi-affine function\non a rectangle has the same minimum on the rectangle as on its\nvertex set. Definition 2.1 means degree at most one in each separate\nvariable, with mixed products allowed. The preceding paragraph\nexplains the convex-combination argument. The exact PDF was\ndownloaded into downloads/1012.1256.pdf and the relevant definitions\nand argument were read through the web PDF text extractor after\nlocal pdftotext failed with \"command not found\". No package was\ninstalled. Equation (11) gives the full needed proof independently;\nno LP or polynomial computation from that paper was run or invoked.\n\nAdversarial checks were symbolic: both row orientations, all remote\nrows, both optimum rows, terminal eta=1, zero/fractional/one tail\ncoefficients, positive slack, the true polar minimum set, the last\ncheckpoint optimizer, arbitrary checkpoint subsets, and the\nfixed-eta counterexample (24). No numerical evaluation, solver,\nmachine algebra, replay, verifier call, consult, or subagent was used.\n\nOne allocated round remains AFTER this round actually returns. The\nspecific positive-tail family (1), even with arbitrary positive\nslack and the exceptional coefficient in (25), is now closed at\ndraft level and must not be retuned. A genuinely different next law\nmust change its dependence or value/terminal geometry. A concrete\nnext candidate is a TWO-future-mode backward dependence\ng_i=t_i g_(i+1)+u_i g_(i+2)+v_i e_i, with mixed signs allowed and\nthe original full normal inequalities retained. Unlike (1), its\ncurrent reserve and radius state need the independent correlation\n<x_(i+1),g_(i+2)> and cross-gradient term; the three-scalar\nseparate-affinity argument does not apply automatically. This is a\nnext research direction, not a proved legal or competitive family.\n\nEven a competitive construction on all original H_m would leave the\nall-n/all-nonnegative-word bridge to the original Goal. Neither that\nbridge nor a full-class upper improvement is supplied here. Counterexample search, exact scope and handoff\n\nTextual attacks on the main extension included both minimum-level\nbranches mu_0<=0 and mu_0>0; the equality triangle-collapse case;\nbeta changing sign; H=0; kappa=0; both lower-radius branches; and the\ncase of a nonpositive final r/q loss rate. Then\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) V_m^obt/K_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=13/10,\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) V_m^obt<=13/[5 exp(1)]<1. (0)\n\nHere the COMPLETE same-word K equals COMPLETE F_ray and is dominated\nby both full Huber-augmented complex/lens references. Section6 gives\nthe stronger finite factor133613/134064<1 for all parameters in one\nstructural pair class.
