The whole family nevertheless misses the\noriginal profile by a fixed relative asymptotic amount. This is an\nadversary-family obstruction, not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.\n\n## 1. Exact declared family and every physical support test\n\nFix the ORIGINAL word h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2). Put\n\n    S_j=sum block_j, T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2,\n    A=b-1>0, E=d-1>0, sigma=2N-1.\n\nBlocks contain their actual chronological physical steps, including zeros.\nThese letters are local abbreviations, not the original B_n benchmark.\nChoose independent u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,beta>=0,kappa>=0,w>0,ell>0. Set\n\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha, H=Mv+beta,\n    p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(-w,ell),\n    a_q=u^2+v^2, a_r=w^2+ell^2, c=<q,r>=uw-v ell,\n    z=(Eu-alpha,Ev-beta), eta=w alpha-ell beta,\n    R_q=u alpha+v beta-kappa, G=R_q-eta.\n\nHere eta is the r-plane height at the ending middle proximal point;\nit is not a free reserve. Define one static finite affine maximum\n\n    J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},\n    F(x)=min_y {J(y)+||x-y||^2/2}, X_0=(T+delta,-H).       (1)\n\nThe complete endpoint tests for the stated constant gradients p,q,r are\n\n    delta>=0, vH<=(1+u)delta+kappa, R_q>=0;              (2)\n    G>=0, G+(M-1)(a_q-c)>=0;                            (3)\n    <r,z>-N a_r>=0;                                    (4)\n    <r-q,z>+kappa-(a_r-c)>=0,\n    <r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c)>=0;                          (5)\n    <r-p,z>-N(a_r+w)>=0.                               (6)\n\nUnlike the positive-quadrant case, (3) is not automatic, and the rate\na_r-c in (5) is not presumed positive. Both actual endpoints are kept.\nThe prefix r comparison is automatic: at its proximal point\ny=(delta+S_0-t,-H), the p-minus-r margin is\n(1+w)(delta+S_0-t)+ell H>0 for every physical t in[0,S_0].\nThe p-versus-zero and p-versus-q margins are minimized at t=S_0;\ntheir values are exactly the first two conditions in (2).\n\nCheckpoint b sends the iterate to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At middle\ncumulative mass t in[0,S_1] its proposed proximal point is\n\n    y=(-alpha,-beta)+(S_1-t)q.\n\nThe q-versus-zero margin is R_q+(S_1-t)a_q>=0. Its margin over p\nis nonnegative because p's height is nonpositive. Its margin over r\nis G+(S_1-t)(a_q-c), so (3) proves every physical middle query.\nThe second checkpoint sends the iterate to z. At tail cumulative\nmass t in[0,S_2], put k=1+t, so y=z-k r with1<=k<=N. The r-minus-zero\nmargin has positive loss rate a_r; the r-minus-p margin has positive\nloss rate a_r+w. Their final tests are (4),(6). The r-minus-q margin\nhas rate a_r-c of unrestricted sign, so (5) proves every physical tail\nquery. This includes the actual terminal point.\n\nThese tests are necessary as well as sufficient for the specified\ngradients of (1). A subgradient of a finite affine maximum is a convex\ncombination of its active slopes: otherwise a separating direction\ncontradicts its directional derivative, the maximum active directional\nslope. The slopes p,q,r are uniquely exposed by directions(1,0),(0,-1),\n(0,1), respectively. Hence their occurrence as subgradients requires\ntheir respective planes to be active. Evaluating activity at the stated\nendpoints gives exactly (2)--(6).\n\n## 2. Static regularity and the actual nearest minimizer\n\nJ is continuous, finite, convex and nonnegative. Its proximal objective\nis coercive and strictly convex, hence has a unique minimizer y(x).\nComparison with y+t(v-y), convexity and t decreasing to0 show\ng(x)=x-y(x) belongs to the subdifferential of J at y(x). Conversely,\nthat subgradient inequality and the quadratic expansion prove proximal\noptimality. Adding the subgradient inequalities at two points gives\n||g(x)-g(x')||^2<=<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>. In particular g is1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluating the proximal objective at the old minimizer gives the upper\nfirst-order bound for F. Applying its subgradient inequality at the new\nminimizer and completing the quadratic gives the lower bound. Thus\n\n    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.\n\nThis proves that F is convex, differentiable and1-smooth with gradient g.\nIts minimum is0 and its minimum set is exactly\n\n    K={x_1<=0, -w x_1+ell x_2<=0, -u x_1-v x_2<=kappa}. (7)\n\nIndeed J=0 gives F=0, and an attained zero proximal objective forces\nboth nonnegative terms to vanish. The geometry in (7) has been rederived\nfor w>0: it lies below the line x_2=(w/ell)x_1<=0 and above\nx_2=(-kappa-u x_1)/v>=-kappa/v. It is a triangle when kappa>0 and\nthe singleton0 when kappa=0. Its left vertex has\nx_1=-kappa/(u+vw/ell); the denominator is positive even when u=0.\n\nSince T+delta>0 and H>0, the closest point to X_0 is\n\n    t_star=min(H,kappa/v), x_star=(0,-t_star),\n    D=(H-kappa/v)_+,\n    R^2=dist(X_0,K)^2=(T+delta)^2+D^2.                  (8)\n\nProof: every point of K has first coordinate<=0 and second coordinate\n>=-kappa/v. These separate lower bounds on squared coordinate distances\nare simultaneously attained at the displayed point, which satisfies all\nthree inequalities of (7). This includes both positive-part branches.\nHere R denotes this local true distance, not the project's ratio in W=2R.\n\n## 3. All directed rows, optimum slacks, payoff and holding price\n\nAssign slopes0,p,q,r affine costs0,0,kappa,0, respectively. At any of\nthe physical samples, activity at y_i=x_i-g_i gives\n\n    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j<=0. Its zero plane is active,\nso (9) also proves both optimum orientations. No interpolation row is\ndeleted and no Gram-only realization is substituted for the function.\n\nThe ending-query affine reserves are delta,R_q,L_0, with\n\n    L_0=<r,z>-N a_r,\n    L_p=<r-p,z>-N(a_r+w),\n    L_q=<r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c).\n\nThe optimum slacks k_i-<g_i,x_star> are0,kappa-v t_star,ell t_star.\nThey are nonnegative and are distinct from the affine reserves.\nThe exact true-distance payoff is\n\n    V=2F(X_n)/R^2=P/R^2,\n    P=2<r,z>-sigma a_r\n     =2eta-2E c-sigma a_r>0.                           (10)\n\nIts positivity follows from (4): P=2L_0+a_r>=a_r>0.\nReplacing F by L F for arbitrary L>0 keeps the same trajectory under\nthe original steps h_i/L and the same normalized payoff.\n\nFor an appended nonnegative list of total mass tau, the SAME function\nholds gradient r at every appended physical query exactly while\n\n    0<=tau<=min{L_0/a_r, L_p/(a_r+w),\n               L_q/(a_r-c) if a_r>c, otherwise +infinity}. (11)\n\nThe three margins become L_0-tau a_r, L_p-tau(a_r+w),\nL_q-tau(a_r-c). A nonpositive loss rate imposes no upper limit.\nExposedness of r supplies necessity. During this exact interval\nV(tau)=V(0)-2tau a_r/R^2. No reserve by itself licenses continuation.\n\n## 4. Positive residual is paid twice; a conditional complete comparison\n\nPut eta_+=max(eta,0). From R_q>=0 and G>=0 one has\n\n    kappa<=u alpha+v beta-eta_+.\n\nCombining this with prefix condition (2) gives\n\n    Mv^2+eta_+<=delta+u(A-Mu),\n    D>=(Mv-u alpha/v+eta_+/v)_+. Thus (17) closes a substantial\ndeclared subdomain, but (16) alone does not close its complement.\n\nA second upper bound, valid for EVERY legal obtuse tuple when T>=2A,\nis obtained by dropping the negative terms -2Euw,-2ell beta and\ncompleting w and ell separately:\n\n    V<= [A^2/T^2+E^2 C(T,A,M)]/sigma.                   (18)\n\nHere alpha<=A and R>=T, and (14) with eta_+=0 follows from the weaker\nconditions kappa<=u alpha+v beta. Again this is an upper relaxation.\n\n## 5. Middle-to-terminal transport: the third price cannot be removed\n\nEvaluate the retained q-plane margin at the actual terminal point:\n\n    L_q=E(a_q-c)-N(a_r-c)-G>=0.\n\nTogether with G>=0 this gives the necessary inequality\n\n    N a_r<=(E) a_q+(N-E)c. Joint proof of the full-norm inequality\n\nThe following calculation is included in full and rechecked for the\nrelaxed variables used in Section4. It is not an invocation of C104 as a fact.\nEquation labels in this appendix carry the prefix A.\n\n\nWe prove from scratch: for A,M,T>0 with T>=2A, u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,\n\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha>=0,\n    Mv^2<=delta+u(A-Mu),\n\none has\n\n    (u^2+v^2)/[(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2]\n       <=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.                 (A15)\n\nThis controls the horizontal contribution that the former orthogonal\nargument did not need. It includes both nearest-minimizer branches.\n\nFirst suppose M<=A+2A^2/T. Put P=A-Mu and d=delta, so0<=d<=P.\nThe constraint gives M(u^2+v^2)<=Au+d. Direct expansion yields\n\n    A^2(T+d)^2-M T^2(Au+d)\n       =A P T^2+(2A^2T-MT^2)d+A^2d^2>=0.              (A16)\n\nIf the coefficient of d is nonnegative, every term is nonnegative.\nIf it is negative, substitute d<=P in just that term; the result is\nat least P[(A-M)T^2+2A^2T]+A^2d^2>=0 by this regime's bound on M.\nDropping the second nonnegative radius square now proves the first\nalternative A^2/(M^2T^2) in (A15). No saturation or endpoint limit is\nneeded in this regime.\n\nSuppose next M>A+2A^2/T. Then M>A. For fixed u,alpha put\n\n    P=A-Mu, d=P-alpha, k=1+u, c=uP,\n    x=v^2, c_0=u alpha/M, H_0=T+d.\n\nLegal v>0 forces P>0. The ratio on the left of (A15) is\n\n    (u^2+x)/[H_0^2+M^2(x-c_0)_+^2/x].                  (A17)\n\nOn x<=c_0 it increases. On x>=c_0, differentiation has the sign of\n\n    H_0^2-2M^2c_0-M^2u^2\n      +2M^2c_0^2/x+M^2u^2c_0^2/x^2.\n\nThe first three terms are at least\n\n    T^2-2Mu(A-Mu)-M^2u^2\n       =T^2-A^2+(A-Mu)^2>=0.\n\nThe branches agree at x=c_0. Therefore (A17) is bounded by its value\nat the allowed endpoint x=(d+c)/M. There\nMx-u alpha=(1+u)d>=0, giving the bound\n\n    F_u(d)=(Au+d)/\n       { M[(T+d)^2+M(1+u)^2d^2/(d+c)] }, 0<=d<=P.     (A18)\n\nThis saturation is only an upper relaxation; no final activity is assumed.\n\nWrite b_0=Au. For u>0, c>0, differentiation of (A18) has the sign of\n\n    G_u(d)=(T+d)(T-d-2b_0)-M(1+u)^2 H_u(d),\n    H_u(d)=d[(b_0+c)d+2b_0c]/(d+c)^2.\n\nExplicitly\n\n    H_u'(d)=2c^2(d+b_0)/(d+c)^3>=0,\n    G_u'(d)=-2(d+b_0)-M(1+u)^2H_u'(d)<=0,\n    G_u(P)=(T+A)(T-A-2Au).                            (A19)\n\nThe last identity follows by substituting P=A-Mu and d+c=P(1+u):\nthe subtracted term at P is Mu(A+P+2Au), which combined with the\nfirst product leaves exactly the displayed factorization.\n\nIn the present regime\n\n    u<=A/M<T/(T+2A)<=(T-A)/(2A).\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent to (T-2A)(T+A)>=0. Hence\nG_u(P)>=0. Because G_u is nonincreasing, G_u(d)>=G_u(P)>=0 throughout\n[0,P]; thus F_u(d) is nondecreasing. When u=0, its formula reduces to\nd/{M[(T+d)^2+Md]}, whose derivative has the sign T^2-d^2>0 for\n0<d<=A. Its limit at d=0 is0. These observations also handle all\nrelaxed endpoint degeneracies; the actual constraint always has v>0.\n\nIt follows that (A18) is at most its alpha=0 value at d=P. Writing\nt=P in[0,A], that value is\n\n    [A^2+(M-A)t]/[M^2(T^2+(2T+A+M)t)].                 (A20)\n\nThis is a quotient of positive affine functions of t. Its derivative\nhas the sign of\n\n    (T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)].\n\nIf nonpositive, the maximum is its value at t=0, namely A^2/(M^2T^2).\nIf nonnegative, the maximum is at t=A and equals\nA/{M[(T+A)^2+MA]}. In the latter case\n\n    M[(T+A)^2+MA]-A(T+A+M)^2\n       =(T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0,\n\nso the value at t=A is at most1/(T+A+M)^2. This proves (A15).\nEvery relaxation, both radius branches, and the u=0 and boundary cases\nhave been retained. This is exactly the full-norm inequality required in Section4 of this joint draft.\n\n\n## Appendix B. (B23)\n\nThe constants satisfy\n\n    q_pair D_pair<=2(29/20)(1/2)^2+(81/400)(1/2)\n                  =661/800,\n    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho)+1/c_r<=199/60<4.\n\nThe actual M_m is at least16: its word has2^m-1 steps, each greater\nthan1. At their common crossover,\n\n    R_pair^2/(2N-1)<=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair).\n\nRelative to the SAME-word canonical Huber value, this gives\n\n    [R_pair^2/(2N-1)]/[1/(2M_m-1)]\n       <=(661/800)(2M_m-1)/(2M_m-199/60)\n       <=(661/800)*31/(32-199/60)=61473/68840. The original all-split B_n, without changing the denominator\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d, put C_n=1+S_circle(n),\nB_n=1+2S_bullet(n), C_0=B_0=1, and\n\n    C_n=max_(k+l+1=n)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n)G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nWe give the sharper elementary upper estimate needed here, rather than\nusing an unverified asymptotic of the benchmark. Full obtuse-family comparison on ALL remaining original pairs\n\n### C.1. A reserve bound retaining the actual first-prefix ratio\n\nFor this appendix only put r_0=A/T and e_0=E/N. These are positive\nscalars, not the terminal vector r. Suppose r_0,e_0<=1/2. Define\n\n    C_res(r_0,e_0)=\n      r_0 sqrt(e_0)/[sqrt(1+e_0 r_0^2)+r_0 sqrt(e_0)],\n            if r_0^2<=e_0/(1+2e_0);\n      (r_0^2+e_0)/[2(1+e_0)], otherwise.              (C1)\n\nThen for eta>0,\n\n    2M eta/R^2<=C_res(r_0,e_0).                       (C2)\n\nHere is the complete derivation. If eta<=u alpha, the calculation in\nSection5 gives2M eta/R^2<=r_0^2/2. This is a precise ansatz obstruction, not a full-cone exclusion or target solution.", "evidence": "# Variable-step dense certificates and a complete alternating-family obstruction\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round36,\nthe second and final analytical round allocated after completed34 by\nTASK.md and master_guidance604b16e76c7981e3. Scope update\nc3f16cd4617d4d93 and source-scope note60f0c805e0e856b2 leave that\nceiling unchanged. The latter's required ordered-Q translation and\nfresh variable-step proof are supplied explicitly below. No numerical evaluation,\nsolver, machine algebra, replay, formal-verifier call, installation,\nconsult or subordinate agent was used. The original Goal remains OPEN.\n\nThe frozen pairs remain r=5,b=1 and r=6,b=1. Here b in the phrase\n\"ray b=1\" is the integer ray parameter; the alternating step excess\ncalled b below is a different, explicitly local real parameter.\n\nAccepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750, Sections2,3,10--13, for the\nbenchmark recurrences, balanced primitive scalar rule, full original\ncertificate identity, physical joins and substitution; and\nfact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original smooth interpolation convention.\nNo unverified shared result is a premise. All new claims used together\nare proved in this single draft. The earlier round35 triangle and\ntwo-tight-cut arguments are not used.\n\nThis work changes the attempted certificate mechanism. Both strict\ntriangular halves of the auxiliary multiplier array are factorized,\nwith factors depending on the actual variable steps. The whole matrix\nremainder is zero, and every ordered multiplier is retained. Two\nnonconstant, nonbasic physical words have complete strictly positive\narrays of this kind. They are weak seeds: the ENTIRE alternating\nspecialization of this declared positive-factor, zero-remainder family\nhas capacity below the appropriate benchmark. This is an obstruction\nto the declared ansatz, not to every full certificate or every seed.\n\nAll new local notation in this document has the implicit suffix _ls36.\nIn particular U_i,D_i below are factor sequences, not the benchmark\nU_n^ZJ or any earlier trajectory bound. P_i is prefix capacity, R_i\nis a reciprocal product, and p_i is an auxiliary scalar product.\n\n## 1. Original certificate, orientation, and the chosen family\n\nFor a word h of length r, let s=sum_(i<r)h_i and A=1+s. Retain all\nr+1 independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r and all formal values f_i.\nSet\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.\n\nThe accepted full certificate interface is\n\n Lambda_ij>=0 (i!=j), Lambda_ii=0,\n e=(1,...,1)^T,\n T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)),\n M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).       (1)\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 proves the original-space identity\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.      (2)\n\nIntroduce the differently oriented residual\n\n Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nWe search for an EXACT identity\n\n                       V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.          (3)\n\nThen the original array is Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, not 2mu_ij, and its\nremainder is M_prim=0 on the whole original space.\n\nThe declared family has strictly positive excesses t_i=h_i-1, i<r.\nChoose positive U_0,...,U_(r-1) and D_1,...,D_r, set U_-1=0 and\nD_(r+1)=0. Set t_r=0 and h_r=1 solely as auxiliary placeholders in\nexpressions multiplied by D_(r+1) or an empty sum. They are not an\nextra physical step. For every 0<=i<j<=r define\n\n mu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),\nmu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)).                      (4)\n\nEquivalently, the original ordered entries are, for every i<j,\n\n Lambda_ij=2(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),\n Lambda_ji=2(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)).                 (4a)\n\nThese formulas define EVERY off-diagonal entry, and mu_ii=0. The\nfamily to be excluded below consists of all choices of these positive\nfactors for which all entries are nonnegative and identity(3) holds.\nIt is not assumed that every original certificate admits this form.\n\nNonnegative entries in(4) force U_i>=U_(i-1) and D_j>=D_(j+1):\nuse j=r in the first formula, and i=0 in the second, respectively.\nConversely these two monotonicities make every entry nonnegative.\n\n## 2. Why the variable-step factorization cancels all mixed terms\n\nFor i<j the two telescoping sums are\n\n sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),\n sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1).\n\nThe coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in sum mu Qbar is exactly\n\n 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)\n -2h_i sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)+2h_j sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i).           (5)\n\nExpansion of(4) gives\n\n mu_ij+mu_ji\n =h_i U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1))\n       -h_j(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1),\n\nso(5) is zero for every pair. This calculation allows unequal steps;\nit is not an appeal to the constant-step source theorem.\n\nLet d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji), so that the coefficient of f_i is\n2d_i. For i<r put p_(i+1)=U_i D_(i+1), with p_0=0. The weights\nentering i from smaller and larger indices are, respectively,\n\nI_i=U_(i-1)(D_i+t_i D_(i+1)) for 1<=i<r,\n J_i=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(i+1),\n\nwith I_0=0. Therefore\n\n              t_i J_i-I_i=t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i.                (6)\n\nIf d_i=h_i, the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 is\n\n -d_i-2I_i-2J_i+2h_i J_i\n                   =-h_i+2(t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i).             (7)\n\nAt the terminal, d_r=-s, the incoming weight is p_r, and its squared\ngradient coefficient is s-2p_r. These formulas also directly check the\nterminal orientation of the original array.\n\n## 3. Complete variable-step construction with explicit sign tests\n\nFor given t_i>0 define\n\n P_k=1+sum_(i<k)(1+t_i),\n R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1),\n p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2,       0<=k<=r.\n\nThus P_0=R_0=1 and P_r=A. Assume\n\n R_r=P_r,\n R_k>1 and P_k>R_k for 1<=k<r.                             (8)\n\nSet U_0=1, and for 1<=k<r set\n\n U_k/U_(k-1)\n   = (P_k+R_k)(1+R_(k+1))/((1+R_k)(P_k-R_k)),\n D_k=p_k/U_(k-1) for 1<=k<=r.                              (9)\n\nAll these factors are positive. Their successive D ratios are\n\n D_(k+1)/D_k\n   = (P_k-R_k)(R_(k+1)-1)/((R_k-1)(P_k+R_k)).              (10)\n\nFor the exact sign check, U_k>=U_(k-1) and D_k>=D_(k+1) are equivalent\nto the following two scalar inequalities for each 1<=k<r:\n\n -R_k(2+R_k+R_(k+1))\n       <=P_k(R_(k+1)-R_k)\n       <=R_k(R_(k+1)+R_k-2).                               (11)\n\nNo matrix PSD test is being replaced by these inequalities: the FULL\nmatrix will be proved identically zero. Inequalities(11) concern all\nof the actual coefficient signs of this particular factorization.\n\nHere is the complete value-flow verification. Define\n\n u_k=(sum_(i<k)h_i U_i)/U_k,\n v_k=(sum_(j=k+2)^r h_(j-1)D_j)/D_(k+1).\n\nThe exact identities are\n\n u_k=(P_k-R_k)/(1+R_(k+1)),\n v_k=(P_(k+1)-R_(k+1))/(R_(k+1)-1).                        (12)\n\nFor u, start from u_0=0 and use\nu_k=(U_(k-1)/U_k)(u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)). Inserting the preceding\nformula for u_(k-1) gives\nu_(k-1)+h_(k-1)=(P_k+R_k)/(1+R_k), and then(9) proves(12).\nFor v, start from v_(r-1)=0, which agrees with R_r=P_r, and use\nv_(k-1)=(D_(k+1)/D_k)(h_k+v_k). The factor h_k+v_k equals\n(P_k+R_k)/(R_(k+1)-1). Substitution in(10) proves the asserted\nformula for v_(k-1). Thus both identities hold, including the endpoints.\n\nThe sum of the flows over indices 0,...,k telescopes across that cut:\n\n sum_(i<=k)d_i\n   =p_(k+1)(1-t_k+v_k-u_k)=P_(k+1)-1.                     (13)\n\nTo check the last equality explicitly, write R=R_(k+1), P=P_k,\nt=t_k, so that R_k=tR and P_(k+1)=P+1+t. Then\n\n p_(k+1)(v_k-u_k)\n   =[(R+1)(P+1+t-R)-(R-1)(P-tR)]/2\n   =P+t+(t-1)(R^2-1)/2.\n\nAdding p_(k+1)(1-t) leaves P+t=P_(k+1)-1, as claimed.\nDifferences of(13) give d_i=h_i for i<r, and total flow zero gives\nd_r=-s. The function coefficients are now all correct.\n\nThe definition of R gives\n\n        t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i=(1-t_i^2)/2.\n\nEquation(7) becomes -h_i+1-t_i^2=-h_i t_i, the required coefficient\nin V_h. At the terminal, R_r=A gives p_r=(A^2-1)/2, so\ns-2p_r=-sA. Section2 cancels all mixed products. This proves(3) as\na polynomial identity in ALL values and ALL original gradients.\n\nConsequently(8)--(11), together with(4) and(9), supply a full original\ncertificate with Lambda=2mu^T and M_prim=0, with no equality or\nidentification of formal coordinates. Strict inequalities in the two\nfactor monotonicities give strictly positive entries on all r(r+1)\nordered off-diagonal pairs.\n\n## 4. Necessary restrictions on the ENTIRE declared factorized family\n\nThis section does not assume the special construction(8)--(9). Start\nwith ANY positive factors in(4) for which all entries are nonnegative\nand the original flow and zero-remainder identity hold.\n\nThe value coefficients give d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s. Comparing(7)\nwith the desired -h_i t_i yields\n\n             t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1,   p_0=0.           (14)\n\nAt the terminal, s-2p_r=-sA gives 2p_r+1=A^2. Multiplying(14)\ntherefore proves the necessary exact balance\n\n                         A product_(i<r)t_i=1.             (15)\n\nThe positive factors make p_1=U_0D_1>0, so(14) at i=0 forces\n0<t_0<1. When r>=2 define\n\n z=(sum_(j=2)^r h_(j-1)D_j)/D_1>0.\n\nThe first value flow is h_0=p_1(1-t_0+z). Substitution of\np_1=(t_0^(-2)-1)/2 gives\n\n                z=(t_0^2+2t_0-1)/(1-t_0)>0.\n\nIt follows that EVERY member of this declared family with r>=2 obeys\n\n                         sqrt(2)-1<t_0<1.                  (16)\n\nThis is a necessary condition on this positive-factor representation,\nnot a claimed necessary condition on all primitive schedules.\n\nAs a concrete failed high-mass migration, put t=(1/3,b,1/3,b,1/3),\nwhere b is the unique positive root of 2b^3+7b^2-27=0. The polynomial\nis increasing on b>0 and has values -9/2 at 3/2 and 46/27 at 5/3.\nThus 3/2<b<5/3, A=7+2b>10, and(15) holds exactly. Nevertheless the\nrequired z above would be -1/3. It cannot equal a strictly positive\nweighted sum. Unit-quadratic balance and a large capacity alone do\nnot extend the source factorization. This diagnostic is NOT asserted\nto pass every other primitive test or to be a full-cone survivor.\n\n## 5. Scope-complete obstruction at five alternating steps\n\nConsider every member of the declared factorized family whose actual\nphysical word is\n\n                  h=(1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b,1+a),  a,b>0.\n\nHere A=6+3a+2b. Equations(15)--(16) force\n\n         A a^3 b^2=1,       sqrt(2)-1<a<1.                 (17)\n\nWe prove the strict cap\n\n                              A<29/3.                      (18)\n\nSuppose instead A>=29/3 and write b=(A-6-3a)/2. For fixed\na in [sqrt(2)-1,1], the expression\n\n                    F(A,a)=A a^3(A-6-3a)^2/4\n\nis strictly increasing in A on A>=29/3. All factors are positive\nthere. At A=29/3, the derivative of a^3(11/3-3a)^2 has sign\n11/3-5a on this interval: its exact derivative is\n3a^2(11/3-3a)(11/3-5a). Thus its minimum is attained at one of\nthe two endpoints. At a=1, F(29/3,1)=29/27>1. At a=sqrt(2)-1,\ndirect multiplication gives\n\n F(29/3,sqrt(2)-1)=(77285sqrt(2)-109243)/54>1,\n\nbecause\n\n              2*77285^2-109297^2=108241>0.\n\nBoth quantities being compared before squaring are positive. Hence\nF(A,a)>1 throughout the assumed region, contradicting(17). This\nproves(18) for the ENTIRE declared five-step alternating family,\nwith arbitrary positive factors and all of their allowed boundary\nzero multipliers. No recurrence(9) or extra symmetry of the array is\nneeded for this obstruction.\n\n## 6. Scope-complete obstruction at six alternating steps\n\nNow consider every member of the same declared family whose word is\n\n               h=(1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b),  a,b>0.\n\nHere A=7+3a+3b, and(15)--(16) give\n\n            A(ab)^3=1,       sqrt(2)-1<a<1.                (19)\n\nWe prove\n\n                              A<23/2.                      (20)\n\nIf A>=23/2, write b=(A-7)/3-a. For each fixed admissible a,\nA[a((A-7)/3-a)]^3 is strictly increasing in A in this region.\nAt A=23/2 the product a(3/2-a) is a positive concave quadratic\nin a, so its minimum on [sqrt(2)-1,1] occurs at an endpoint.\nThe endpoint a=1 gives A(ab)^3=23/16>1. The other gives\n\n (23/2)[(sqrt(2)-1)(5/2-sqrt(2))]^3\n                    =(54901sqrt(2)-77625)/16>1,\n\nsince\n\n                2*54901^2-77641^2=114721>0.\n\nAgain all compared sides are positive. This contradicts(19), proving\n(20) for the entire declared six-step alternating family.\n\n## 7. Two explicit nonbasic certificates; all 30/42 signs and full PSD\n\nThe exclusions above are not the consequence of an empty ansatz. We\nnow give one complete nonconstant algebraic certificate at each length.\nFormulas(4),(8)--(9) are finite explicit formulas for EVERY multiplier;\nno linear system, optimizer or uncomputed PSD condition remains.\n\nFive steps: set a=2/3 and let b be the unique positive root of\n\n                         16b^3+64b^2-27=0.\n\nThe polynomial is strictly increasing on b>0. Its values at 3/5 and\n5/8 are -63/125 and 61/32, so\n\n       3/5<b<5/8,\n       h=(5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b,5/3),\n       A=8+2b,       46/5<A<37/4.                          (21)\n\nThe polynomial equation is exactly A(2/3)^3 b^2=1, hence R_5=A.\nDefine the factors by(9) and all 30 original multipliers by(4) and\nLambda_ij=2mu_ji.\n\nSix steps: set a=2/3 and let b be the unique positive root of\n\n                          8b^4+24b^3-9=0.\n\nIts derivative is positive on b>0. At b=2/3 the value is -25/81;\nat b=7/10 it is 1441/1250. Therefore\n\n       2/3<b<7/10,\n       h=(5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b),\n       A=9+3b,       11<A<111/10.                          (22)\n\nIts defining equation is exactly A[(2/3)b]^3=1, hence R_6=A.\nUse(9),(4) to define all 42 original multipliers.\n\nWe check EVERY hypothesis in Section3 with exact bounds. In both\nexamples all t_i lie in (or at the interior rational point of)\n[3/5,7/10], and R_k>1 because t_i<1. The strict prefix inequalities\nP_k>R_k in the five-step case follow from\n\n P_1=8/3>3/2=R_1,\n P_2>64/15>5/2>R_2,\n P_3>89/15>15/4>R_3,\n P_4-R_4=(A-5)/3>0.\n\nIn the six-step case they follow from\n\n P_1=8/3>3/2=R_1,\n P_2>13/3>9/4>R_2,\n P_3>6>27/8>R_3,\n P_4>23/3>81/16>R_4,\n P_5-R_5=(1-b)A-(1+b)>8/5.\n\nThe last inequalities use terminal balance, not an approximate root.\nThus every denominator in(9)--(10) is strictly positive.\n\nBecause R_(k+1)>R_k, the first inequality in(11) is strict. For\nthe second inequality define\n\n E_k=(1+t_k)R_k-(1-t_k)P_k-2t_k.\n\nIt is exactly the second sign inequality after multiplication by\npositive factors. At k=1, direct substitution in both examples gives\n\n                       E_1=(13b-7)/6>2/15.\n\nFor k>=2 the common rational interval yields\n\n E_k >= H_k:=(8/5)(10/7)^k-9/5-17k/25.\n\nHere R_k>=(10/7)^k, P_k<=1+17k/10, 1+t_k>=8/5,\n1-t_k<=2/5 and 2t_k<=7/5. The lower bound is strictly positive:\n\n H_2=129/1225>0,\n H_(k+1)-H_k=(24/35)(10/7)^k-17/25>=1/175>0.\n\nConsequently U_0>0, all successive U differences are positive,\nall successive D differences are positive, and D_r>0=D_(r+1).\nEvery one of the 30 or 42 original off-diagonal entries is STRICTLY\npositive. Section3 proves the exact flows and identity(3), so in the\nnotation of(1)\n\n                         M_prim=0 on R^6 or R^7.            (23)\n\nThis is a full-space PSD statement, not a compressed Hessian test.\nThere are no zero physical steps in these examples and no coordinates\nwere deleted.\n\nThese words are genuinely nonbasic in the exact ConPP grammar of\naccepted04219554e41d9750. (24)\n\nBut a nonempty word obtained by an exact ConPP join at its root has\nsome bridge h_j=1+d with dA=PD, by the defining identity in the\naccepted join. Equation(24) rules out every such root. The\npolynomial for Omega(4,29/5) has value -23/80 at X=47/4,\nso its positive root exceeds 47/4. Monotonicity of Omega yields\n\n              C_6>47/4>23/2,\n              C_6/(23/2)>47/46=1+1/46.                   (26)\n\nThe ray lower comparison needed here is\n\n                         Z_m>=C_(m-1) Z_1                 (27)\n\nfor every positive integer m. Replace EACH primitive empty leaf by an original maximizing\nprimitive tree of length m-1 and its ONE dominant empty leaf by a\nmaximizing dominant tree of that same length. Homogeneity and\nmonotonicity at each join give\n\n                    B_(m(n+1)-1)>=C_(m-1) B_n.\n\nTake n=2^j-1, divide by lambda^j, and use the accepted integer-ray\nlimits. This proves(27). Thus every actual seed in the declared\nalternating families satisfies the STRICT WRONG-WAY comparisons\n\n r=5: Z_6/(A Z_1)>1+569/580000,\n r=6: Z_7/(A Z_1)>1+1/46.                                 (28)\n\nIn particular neither family can satisfy the required A>Z_6/Z_1 or\nA>Z_7/Z_1 on the frozen b=1 rays. These comparisons do not transfer\nthe four-step193/25 threshold or assume any unproved phase optimum.\n\nThere is a stronger finite physical statement. Let h be ANY actual\ncertificate from one of these families. Apply the accepted physical\nsubstitution to a fixed maximizing B_n tree, using this seed of\ncapacity A. Keep the ONE dominant empty leaf untouched. The actual\nword H_A has length mn, with m=r+1, and exact all-function value\n\n                          W_(mn)(H_A)=1/D_A.\n\nLet C=C_r. The accepted original-coordinate joins and final square\nremain in both constructions. Since C>A, monotonicity at the joins\ngives D_C>D_A for every n>=1. (30)\n\nThis pays for the unchanged dominant leaf explicitly. Both(25) and\n(26) imply C/A>1+1/2000. On n=2^j-1, the accepted elementary\nbound B_n>=2n+1 and 2^30>10^9 imply, for every j>=30,\nB_n>400000000, hence B_n^(-1/2)<1/20000. Inserting this in(30)\nand using\n\n (1+1/2000)(1-1/20000)^2\n   >(1+1/2000)(1-1/10000)>1+1/4000\n\nproves the uniform finite statement\n\n W_(m(2^j-1))(H_A)\n       >(1+1/4000) U_(m(2^j-1))^ZJ,  EVERY integer j>=30,\n       m=6 or m=7 respectively.                            (31)\n\nThe actual horizons are 6*2^j-6 and 7*2^j-7. This is a deficit of\nthis explicit seed-substitution mechanism, not a lower bound against\narbitrary schedules and not a solution of the original Goal.\n\n## 9. Original full-space identity and elementary boundary consequences\n\nFor h=(h0,h1,h2,h3)>=0 put s=sum h_i, C=1+s, and\nP_i=sum_(j<i)h_j. Indices of all five independent formal gradients are\n0,1,2,3,4. Let e be the column of five ones, and use the original matrix\n\n    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).\n\nFor a zero-diagonal array Lambda, let L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda and\nM=D+sym(LT), where sym(A)=(A+A^T)/2. The original sufficient condition\nis Lambda_ij>=0 for all i!=j, L^T e=omega, and M PSD on ALL R5.\nThe accepted identity is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g0,...,g4)>,        (1)\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    x_i=x0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                             -s(s+1)||g4||^2.\n\nActual smooth convex trajectories satisfy every Q_ij<=0. The sign\norientation and the factor in (1) are the ones in accepted04219554e41d9750.\n\nEvery correct flow has e^T M e=0. Indeed Te=e+P,\n2sum h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2,\ne^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2, and omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2.\nPSD therefore forces Me=0, by evaluating (e+az)^T M(e+az) for both\nsigns of small a. Under the flow this gives\n\n    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i), i<4,\n    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                         (2)\n\nFor example, (Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 at i<4, while\n(Me)_4=-sC+(LP)_4/2. These expansions also prove (2) directly.\n\nFor s>0 both endpoint steps obey\n\n    h0,h3 <= 2C/(C+1)<2.                                  (3)\n\nFor h0>1, (2) gives 2h0<=s out0, and the first diagonal gives\nh0(2-h0)>=(h0-1)out0. Combining proves the first inequality;\nh0<=1 is already below the bound. For h3>0, the terminal diagonal\ngives out4>=s(s+2), and (2) gives\n2sC>=h3 out4>=h3 s(s+2). The zero case needs no division.\nHere out_i=sum_(j!=i)Lambda_ij denotes the outgoing mass. The same\nproofs apply verbatim to the compressed word at any smaller length.\n\nWe will use the stronger construction requirement M=0. For any queried\nindex i its diagonal is\n\n    M_ii=h_i(2-h_i)+sum_(j<i)Lambda_ij\n                                  +(1-h_i)sum_(j>i)Lambda_ij. (4)\n\nThus a positive h_i<=1 is impossible when all Lambda>=0 and M=0.\nIf h_i=0, (4) forces every outgoing coefficient at i to vanish;\nthe zero flow then forces all incoming coefficients to vanish. Such a\nvertex is isolated. Removing it compresses the zero physical step and\nrestricts the full identity without identifying two formal gradients.\nConversely no compression is used to justify an untested original row.\n\nAt the threshold no zero step is possible in this zero-remainder class.\nHere is a bound sufficient to prove that assertion. For a positive bridge\nstep 1+delta with prefix capacity A and suffix capacity E, put\neta=delta/E>0. Use the scalar function with derivative\nclamp(x,[-eta,1]), starting from x0=A. At the bridge query x=1;\nthe bridge lands at -delta, and the tail ends at -eta, with all its\ngradients -eta. For this function f(x)=x g-g^2/2. Direct substitution\nand the GD telescope give V_h=x0^2-(x_n+s g_n)^2=A^2-C^2 eta^2.\nIts derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, so it is\nan actual convex 1-smooth function; normalize f(0)=0.\nConsequently every feasible array requires\n\n    C delta <= A E.                                       (5)\n\nWith at most two positive steps, (3) on the compressed word gives s<4.\nWith three positive steps, write the middle one as 1+delta. Both\nendpoints are <2, so A,E<3. Equation (5) gives delta<9/C, hence\nC<6+9/C, or C<3+3sqrt(2)<15/2<193/25. The all-zero case is immediate.\nThis proves the asserted zero-step exclusion without dropping a formal\ncoordinate from an allegedly feasible original array.\n\nDefine p0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. Then Tp=e and Le=0. Hence\n\n    p^T M p=p^T Dp=1-C^2 p4^2,                             (6)\n\nwhere the last equality telescopes\nh_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2. When M=0 this is an equality\nC|p4|=1, rather than merely a necessary inequality. In particular,\nafter all four positive steps have been shown >1, put r_i=h_i-1>0;\nthen\n\n    C r0 r1 r2 r3=1.                                      (7)\n\nThe extra quadratic kernel is thus derived on the original full space.\n\n## 2. The declared signed interval family\n\nFor 0<=a<b<=4, let J^[a,b] be zero outside rows/columns [a,b].\nIts off-diagonal entries inside the interval are\n\n    J_ij=(i-a+1)h_i-1           when i<j,\n    J_ij=1+(b-i)h_i             when j<i,                   (8)\n\nand each diagonal is minus its off-diagonal row sum. Put h4=0 solely\nas a convention in (8); its value does not affect the final row of a mode.\nIn particular J e=0 for every mode.\n\nFor local indices a<=i<j<=b, put\nu_i=(i-a+1)h_i-1 and v_j=1+(b-j)h_j. Multiplication gives\n(JT)_ij=u_i v_j and (JT)_ji=-v_j u_i. A column before a vanishes by\nthe zero row sum; a column after b vanishes by triangularity. Therefore\nsym(JT) is diagonal. At i in [a,b] its diagonal is\n\n    (h_i-1)^2(i-a+1)(b-i)-(i-a)(b-i+1).                   (9)\n\nTo check (9) without omitting a term, let l=i-a and m=b-i.\nThe diagonal of JT is\n(h_i-1)m((l+1)h_i-1)-l(1+m h_i)\n=(h_i-1)^2(l+1)m-l(m+1).\n\nThe selected construction is\n\n    N=sum_(a=0)^3 tau_a J^[a,4]\n                        +sum_(i=0)^2 q_i J^[i,i+1],       (10)\n\nwith ALL seven weights tau_a,q_i real. Define actual Lambda_ij=N_ij\nfor i!=j and Lambda_ii=0. Since N e=0, its row Laplacian is L=-N.\nRequire\n\n    N^T e=-omega, sym(NT)=D, Lambda_ij>=0 (i!=j).          (11)\n\nEquations (11) imply M=D-sym(NT)=0 on the entire R5. No smaller\nmatrix, rank heuristic, numerical factorization or dropped coordinate is\nbeing used. Conversely (11) is the precise zero-remainder family (10).\nSigned mode weights are permitted; only the final actual Lambda signs count.\n\n## 3. All seven weights and all twenty entries, explicitly\n\nAssume here h_i>1 for i<4. Define\n\n    S_i=sum_(a=0)^i tau_a, B_i=sum_(j=0)^i S_j, B_(-1)=0,\n    U_i=h_i B_i-S_i=r_i B_i+B_(i-1),\n    V_i=1+(4-i)h_i,\n    K_i=product_(j=0)^i r_j^(-2)-1.                       (12)\n\nB_i here denotes an interval-coordinate cumulative flow, NOT the\nproject's benchmark B_n. In fact the final outgoing terminal coefficients\nare Lambda_4i=S_i, so B_i is their cumulative sum.\n\nThe mode formula gives, for every i<j,\n\n    Lambda_ij=U_i+1_(j=i+1)*r_i*q_i,\n    Lambda_ji=V_j S_i+1_(j=i+1)*q_i,                      (13)\n\nwhere q3=0. Let Z_i=(4-i)B_i+q_i. By (9) the diagonal equation at i<4\nis r_i^2 Z_i-Z_(i-1)=h_i(2-h_i), with Z_(-1)=0. Since\nh_i(2-h_i)=1-r_i^2, this recursion gives Z_i=K_i. The terminal\ndiagonal is -B3, so it gives B3=C^2-1. Consequently the diagonal\nequations are exactly (7) and\n\n    B3=C^2-1, q_i=K_i-(4-i)B_i, i=0,1,2.                (14)\n\nFor the cumulative column flow through cut i, put\nH_i=sum_(j=i+1)^3(4-j)h_j and E_(i-1)=sum_(j<i)h_j B_j.\nUsing (13), the backward mass minus forward mass across that cut is\n\n    [2(4-i)+H_i]B_i-(4-i)sum_(j<=i)h_j B_j\n                                             +(2-h_i)q_i.\n\nSubstitution of (14) reduces this to\n\n    H_i B_i-(4-i)E_(i-1)+(2-h_i)K_i.                     (15)\n\nThe required value is 2P_(i+1). Thus the first three cuts uniquely give\n\n    B0=[2h0-(2-h0)K0]/(3h1+2h2+h3),\n    B1=[2(h0+h1)+3h0 B0-(2-h1)K1]/(2h2+h3),\n    B2=[2(h0+h1+h2)+2(h0 B0+h1 B1)-(2-h2)K2]/h3.         (16)\n\nThere is no remaining linear equation hidden in this solution. Indeed,\ngiven (7), (14), (16), recover\nS_i=B_i-B_(i-1), tau_i=S_i-S_(i-1), with S_(-1)=0.\nAll diagonal equations hold by the displayed recursion, and the first\nthree flow components hold by the first three cuts. If b=N^T e, then\ne^T b=0 and\nb^T Te=e^T NT e=e^T D e=(-omega)^T Te.\nThe error b+omega is therefore zero except possibly at indices3,4,\nwhere it has values delta,-delta. Its inner product with Te=e+P is\n-h3 delta. Since h3>0, delta=0. All five original flows hold.\n\nFor direct use, the twenty entries resulting from (14)--(16) are\n\n    Lambda01=r0(K0-3B0),\n    Lambda02=Lambda03=Lambda04=r0 B0,\n    Lambda10=K0+3r1 B0,\n    Lambda12=r1 K1+B0-2r1 B1,\n    Lambda13=Lambda14=r1 B1+B0,\n    Lambda20=(1+2h2)B0,\n    Lambda21=K1+2r2 B1-(1+2h2)B0,\n    Lambda23=r2 K2+B1-r2 B2,\n    Lambda24=r2 B2+B1,\n    Lambda30=(1+h3)B0,\n    Lambda31=(1+h3)(B1-B0),\n    Lambda32=K2+r3 B2-(1+h3)B1,\n    Lambda34=r3 B3+B2,\n    Lambda40=B0, Lambda41=B1-B0,\n    Lambda42=B2-B1, Lambda43=B3-B2.                       (17)\n\nThis list, its zero diagonal, (7), (14), and (16) specify the entire\noriginal array. Checking the signs in (17) is necessary and sufficient\nwithin (10). In particular, final sign feasibility forces\n\n    0<=B0<=B1<=B2<=B3.                                   (18)\n\nThe first expression in (16) simplifies to\n\n    B0=h0(h0^2-2)/[(h0-1)^2(3h1+2h2+h3)].                (19)\n\nThus every positive feasible member of this particular family has\nh0>=sqrt(2). This is NOT a necessary condition for an arbitrary full\nprimitive certificate; it comes from the seven-mode equations.\n\n## 4. A dense actual calibration below the requested threshold\n\nLet r be the unique positive root of\n\n    r^4(5+4r)=1,\n\nand set h0=h1=h2=h3=t=1+r. The polynomial on the left is strictly\nincreasing for r>0. At r=3/5 it is 2997/3125<1, while at r=61/100\nit is 2575326426/2500000000>1. Hence\n\n    3/5<r<61/100, C=5+4r<186/25<193/25.                 (20)\n\nDefine K_i, B_i and all twenty entries by (12), (14), (16), (17).\nThen (7) holds exactly, and Section3 proves every flow and M=0.\nHere are elementary exact sign checks; no external constant-step theorem\nor numerical feasibility is invoked.\n\nWrite eta=(1-r)/(1+r). From (20),\n\n    6/25<eta<1/4,\n    5/3<K0<16/9, 6<K1<7, 17<K2<21.\n\nFor example r^2<3/8 gives the lower K bounds, and r>3/5 gives\nthe upper bounds. Formula (16) reduces to\n\n    B0=(r^2+2r-1)/(6r^2),\n    B1=4/3+B0-eta K1/3,\n    B2=6+2(B0+B1)-eta K2.\n\nThe derivative of B0 is (1-r)/(3r^3)>0; B0 at 3/5 is 7/27>1/4.\nAlso r^2+2r-1<3/5 and 6r^2>54/25, giving B0<5/18<3/10.\nConsequently\n\n    1/4<B0<3/10,\n    1<B1<173/150<6/5,\n    13/4<B2<123/25<5,\n    B3=C^2-1>1344/25>5.                                 (21)\n\nThese bounds prove all four terminal coefficients and every nonadjacent\nentry in (17) strictly positive. For the other entries:\n\n    K0-3B0>5/3-9/10>0,\n    K1-2B1>6-12/5>0,\n    K2-B2>17-5>0,\n    Lambda10=K0+3r B0>0,\n    Lambda21>6-(211/50)(3/10)>0,\n    Lambda32>17-(261/100)(6/5)>0.\n\nThe last forward coefficient is Lambda34=r B3+B2>0 as well.\nThus all twenty actual off-diagonal coefficients are strictly positive.\nThis is a fully specified algebraic dense certificate, with M=0 on R5.\nIt has capacity below the target, and is not a threshold seed.\n\n## 5. Entire equal-tail family: statement and reductions\n\nClaim. No word h=(u,w,t,t)>=0 with C=1+u+w+2t>=193/25 admits\nany certificate of the form (10)--(11), even with arbitrary signed\ntau_a,q_i.\n\nBy Section1 any putative threshold certificate has all four steps >1.\nWrite\n\n    a=u-1, b=w-1, d=t-1, a0=sqrt(2)-1,\n    C0=193/25, rC=(C-1)/(C+1).\n\nEquations (3), (7), (18), (19) imply\n\n    a0<=a<=rC<1, 0<d<=rC,\n    b=C-5-a-2d>0,\n    C a b d^2=1.                                        (22)\n\nThe proof splits only at d=1/2 and covers both pieces completely.\n\n### 5.1. The whole d>=1/2 piece\n\nOn the rectangle a in[a0,rC], d in[1/2,rC], define\n\n    F_C(a,d)=a d^2(C-5-a-2d).\n\nFor C>=C0, the last factor is positive throughout this rectangle:\nits minimum is C-5-3rC, positive at C0 and increasing thereafter.\nFor fixed d, F_C is a concave quadratic in a, so its minimum is\nat an endpoint of the a interval. At either endpoint a, its derivative\nin d is 2a d(C-5-a-3d), which can change sign only from positive\nto negative. Hence the minimum in d is also at an endpoint.\n\nThe four possible minimum values are\n\n    a0(C-6-a0)/4,\n    rC(C-6-rC)/4,\n    a0 rC^2(C-5-a0-2rC),\n    rC^3(C-5-3rC).                                      (23)\n\nEach is positive and increasing for C>=C0: rC'=2/(C+1)^2,\nso each bracket in (23) has positive derivative, and its positive\nnonconstant prefactors also increase. At C0 use the rational bounds\n\n    41/100<a0<21/50, 3/4<rC0=84/109<4/5.\n\nThey give the following respective lower bounds for (23):\n\n    533/4000, 69/400, 2583/16000, 27/200.\n\nFor the third bracket, for example,\nC0-5-a0-2rC0>68/25-21/50-8/5=7/10.\nFor the fourth it is >68/25-12/5=8/25.\nEvery displayed bound is at least 533/4000. It follows that\n\n    C F_C(a,d) >= (193/25)(533/4000)\n                 =102869/100000>1,\n\ncontradicting (22). This closes the entire d>=1/2 piece.\n\n### 5.2. The whole 0<d<1/2 piece\n\nSince a<1 and 2d<1,\n\n    b>C-7>=18/25>1/2.                                   (24)\n\nThe nonnegative original coefficients Lambda40,Lambda41 give\nB1>=B0>=0. The nonnegative original Lambda12 in (17) gives\n\n    b K1+B0-2b B1>=0,\n\nso K1>=(2-1/b)B0>=0. For equal last steps, (16) says\n\n    3t B1=2(u+w)+3u B0-(2-w)K1.\n\nSubstitute this expression into the Lambda12 inequality. The result is\n\n    (5+3d-2b)K1 >= 4(u+w)+(6u-3t/b)B0.                  (25)\n\nThe last coefficient is positive: u>=sqrt(2)>7/5, b>18/25,\nand t<3/2 imply 2ub>252/125>3/2>t. Thus (25) requires\n\n    (5+3d-2b)K1>=4(u+w)>0.                              (26)\n\nIn particular both factors on the left are positive. By (22),\n\n    K1=1/(a^2 b^2)-1=C^2 d^4-1<C^2/16-1,\n    5+3d-2b<17+7d-2C<41/2-2C,\n    u+w=C-3-2d>C-4.\n\nTherefore (26) would imply\n\n    4(C-4)<(41/2-2C)(C^2/16-1).                         (27)\n\nThis is impossible for EVERY C>=C0. In fact the left side minus the\nright side of (27) is\n\n    g(C)/32, g(C)=4C^3-41C^2+64C+144.\n\nThe exact endpoint values are\n\n    g(193/25)=546003/15625>0,\n    g'(193/25)=91338/625>0,\n\nand g''(C)=24C-82>0 on C>=C0. Thus g is positive on that whole ray.\nThis contradicts (27), closing the 0<d<1/2 piece and proving the claim.\n\nOnly original coefficient signs, original flow, and the full M=0\nidentity were used. The proof permits all seven weights to be signed\nand every final coefficient to lie on its nonnegative boundary.\n\n## 6. Hostile calibration: the seven-mode family misses a valid ConPP word\n\nThe following gives an exact reason not to extend Section5 to all\nzero-remainder certificates or to all four-step words with equal tails.\n\nPut A=1+sqrt(2), let b>0 solve b(A+4+b)=4A, and put\n\n    C=A+4+b, kappa=C^2/16,\n    h=(sqrt(2),1+b,3/2,3/2).                              (28)\n\nThis word has an actual full certificate with M=0. To specify it\nexplicitly, its only nonzero entries are\n\n    Lambda01=2, Lambda10=2A,\n    Lambda14=(kappa-1)b,\n    Lambda21=Lambda31=3(kappa-1)/2,\n    Lambda23=kappa, Lambda24=2kappa-3/2,\n    Lambda32=7kappa/2, Lambda34=10kappa-3/2,\n    Lambda41=kappa-1, Lambda42=kappa, Lambda43=14kappa.     (29)\n\nAll entries not listed are zero, as is the diagonal. All listed entries\nare positive because C>4 and b>0. Here is a full-matrix derivation of\n(29) rather than an inference from a smaller principal block.\n\nThe one-step sqrt(2) certificate has multipliers2,2A and full remainder\n(2-(sqrt(2))^2)(g0-g1)^2=0. The two-step word(3/2,3/2) has\n\n    lambda01=1, lambda02=1/2, lambda10=7/2,\n    lambda12=17/2, lambda20=1, lambda21=14.\n\nIts row Laplacian has flow(-3,-3,6), and direct multiplication with\nT=[[1,0,0],[3/2,1,0],[3/2,3/2,1]] gives\nsym(LT)=-diag(3/4,3/4,-15). Its entire remainder is zero.\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 Section11 embeds these two child remainders,\nmultiplies the right one by kappa, and adds (kappa-1) times the\ndisplayed nonnegative primitive-join interpolation combination. With\nbridge index1 and right-child queries2,3,4, this gives exactly (29).\nHence every original flow is correct and the full five-by-five M is zero.\n\nBut (28) cannot have a certificate in the seven-mode class (10).\nIt satisfies (7), because Cb=4A and A(sqrt(2)-1)=1. Equations (16),\n(19) force B0=0. Let K1=A^2/b^2-1>0; positivity follows from b<A,\nwhich follows from C>4 and bC=4A. Then (16) simplifies to\n\n    B1=2K1/3.\n\nFor explicit verification, its numerator is\n(A+b)[b^2+(A+1)b-A]/b^2;\nthe bracket equals3(A-b) by b^2+(A+4)b=4A, and its denominator\n2h2+h3 is9/2. This gives B1=2K1/3 exactly. Formula (17) then forces\n\n    Lambda12=b K1-2b B1=-b K1/3<0.                       (30)\n\nNo alternative seven weights can repair (30), since Section3 proved\ntheir uniqueness for fixed positive h. The actual certificate (29)\nhas Lambda13=0 and Lambda14>0, whereas (17) requires Lambda13=Lambda14.\nReleasing that specific repeated forward coefficient is therefore a\nnecessary change for this calibration, even with arbitrary signed mode\nweights and a zero full remainder.\n\nThis is a scope counterexample, not a threshold seed: no capacity gain\nbeyond the original basic composition is claimed for (28). Explicitly,\nC is the positive root of C^2-(A+4)C-4A=0. Since A<121/50, this\npolynomial at C0=193/25 is greater than89/250>0, proving C<C0.\n\n### 6.1. An explicit full-matrix repair of that calibration\n\nThe failed relation can actually be released by a displayed homogeneous\ndirection, rather than just named as missing. For h=(u,w,z,v) with\nb=w-1,c=z-1,d=v-1>0, define a skew-symmetric five-by-five matrix\nA_cyc with zero row/column0 and the following upper entries:\n\n    (A_cyc)12=-bcv, (A_cyc)13=b(cd-1), (A_cyc)14=bz,\n    (A_cyc)23=-(1+bcd), (A_cyc)24=1-bc, (A_cyc)34=-w.\n\nLet p_tail=(1,-b,bc,-bcd)^T on indices1,2,3,4. Direct multiplication\ngives A_cyc e=0 and A_cyc(0,p_tail)^T=0. For example the first of\nthese four row sums is -bcv+b(cd-1)+bz=0; the corresponding p_tail\nrow product is b^2 c[v+cd-1-zd]=0. The other three row sums are\nbcv-bcd-1+1-bc, -b(cd-1)+bcd+1-w, and -bz-1+bc+w,\nrespectively, and all vanish. The other three p_tail products are\nbc[v-bcd-1-d(1-bc)],\nb[1-cd-(1+bcd)+wcd], and\nb[-z+1-bc+wc], respectively, and all vanish as well.\n\nFor any real rho put Delta L=rho A_cyc T^(-1). Since T^(-1)e=p\nfrom (6) and the last four entries of p are (1-u)p_tail,\n\n    Delta L e=0, (Delta L)^T e=0,\n    sym(Delta L T)=0.                                   (31)\n\nMoreover column0 of T^(-1), restricted to indices1,...,4, is\n-u p_tail. Thus Delta L has zero row and column0. Its only changed\noff-diagonal multipliers, Delta Lambda_ij=-(Delta L)_ij, are\n\n    Delta Lambda12=-rho b v,\n    Delta Lambda13= rho b(z+v),\n    Delta Lambda14=-rho b z,\n    Delta Lambda21= rho c v,\n    Delta Lambda23= rho(v+1-bc),\n    Delta Lambda24= rho(bc-1),\n    Delta Lambda31= rho(1-cd),\n    Delta Lambda32= rho[d(1+b+c)-1],\n    Delta Lambda34= rho w,\n    Delta Lambda41=-rho z,\n    Delta Lambda42= rho(w+z),\n    Delta Lambda43=-rho w.                              (32)\n\nFor an entrywise check, the tail inverse matrix used to obtain (32) is\n\n    [ 1       0       0    0\n     -w       1       0    0\n      wc     -z       1    0\n     -wcd     zd     -v    1 ].\n\nThus (31)--(32) preserve every original flow and EVERY entry of the\noriginal full M, not just a principal block or one covariance. Actual\nnonnegativity must still be checked after the change. This is not an\nunconditional sign-preserving move.\n\nAt the concrete word (28), put K=kappa-1. The unique seven-mode base is\n\n    B0=0, B1=2K/3,\n    B2=1+(20+2b)K/9, B3=16K+15.\n\nThese formulas follow from (16) and K(4-b)=2(A+b), which in turn\nfollows from Cb=4A and kappa=C^2/16. Select rho=-2K/9 in (32).\nThen Lambda12 changes from -bK/3 to0, Lambda13 changes from2bK/3\nto0, and Lambda14 changes from2bK/3 to bK. The outgoing terminal\nentries become K,1+K,14(1+K) at columns1,2,3. For the other changed\nentries, substitution in (17) and (32) gives, in order21,23,24,31,32,34,\n\n    3K/2, 1+K, 1/2+2K, 3K/2, (7/2)(1+K), 17/2+10K.\n\nTogether with the unchanged entries these are exactly (29), so every\nsign is now nonnegative. This is a constructive repair of the actual\nnegative coefficient, with full-space PSD preserved identically.\nIt demonstrates an essential non-tree skew freedom absent from (10).\nIt supplies no threshold word: the repaired word still has C<C0.\n\n## 7. What is closed, and what remains to do\n\nClosed at joint-draft level: the entire declared seven-mode construction\non every nonnegative equal-tail word(u,w,t,t) with sum>=168/25. The\ncalibration in Section4 has all twenty entries positive and full M=0,\nso the family is an actual dense certificate mechanism. Section6 proves\nits loss of freedom by one explicit negative ORIGINAL multiplier at\na valid full ConPP word, and (31)--(32) repair that entire actual array.\n\nStill open: the broader seven-mode family with unequal tails, the full\nten-interval zero-remainder family, every general nonzero PSD remainder,\nthe original threshold cone, and the original all-word exact-profile Goal.\nThe evidence does not show that a survivor of every old necessary test\nexists, and supplies no such claimed survivor.\n\nIn particular, repeating the variable-step interval identity or changing\nonly the seven weights in the excluded equal-tail family cannot produce\nthe requested seed.
