{"id": "89aa6dc614ddac9f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T19:49:04.277264+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max45 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED original-H_m research: EVERY positive-power nested-normal policy fails an actual three-point cycle, even with freely reassigned values. A different explicit signed H2-block law is fully legal, including negative backward coefficients below -1 and arbitrary variable capped strengths, but its ENTIRE true-radius family has V<=(5/9)^(2^(m-3)-1), so original-B and complete SAME-word K/ray/FULL-complex relative payoffs all tend0. Removing its reserve growth by kappa=s+1 fails the ORIGINAL pair(3,8), even at eta1. Full laws, all physical pairs, exact minima/radius and scope limits supplied; Goal OPEN.", "evidence": "# Nested normals and signed block gradients on the original ruler\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max's second\nand final round in the allocation starting after completed43. The\ncompleted target after return is45 and the attempted ceiling is46,\nincluding the historical interruption. No formal-verifier allowance is\navailable. Nothing here is an accepted fact or a completion of the Goal.\n\nThe original target is unchanged: an asymptotically matching lower bound\nfor every sufficiently large physical horizon and every nonnegative\npredetermined word, or a certified fixed-relative asymptotic full-class\nupper improvement over the ORIGINAL Zhang--Jiang profile. Throughout,\nW=2R: normalized payoff means twice the final gap divided by smoothness\ntimes the squared distance to the entire minimizer set.\n\nAccepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 for the interpolation convention,\nb98bb807d514e80d for the complete K lower bound and original benchmark,\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a for the COMPLETE alternating-ray channel, and\ndfde2fa326c908bf for the FULL schedule-dependent complex channel.\nNo unverified family closure is used as a mathematical premise. Every\nnew ingredient below is proved jointly. In particular the max44 draft\nis motivation, not a predecessor.\n\n## 1. Results and the new dependence\n\nFor each integer m>=4 retain the SAME enclosing parameters everywhere:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S_m=sum h.\n\nThere are three results, with deliberately different scopes.\n\n1. The nested-normal construction of Section2 gives explicit real\n   points and gradients, with a new independent dual-normal state and\n   every chronological forward normal tied. Its natural power policy\n   fails an ORIGINAL three-point interpolation cycle for EVERY p>0\n   and EVERY terminal eta>1 on every H_m,m>=4. This remains a failure\n   if all values are freely reassigned. The more general exact failed\n   hypothesis is a convex increasing inverse-normal profile over the\n   first two physical time intervals, with a positive second increment.\n\n2. The different signed block rule in Section4 IS a legal static\n   smooth-convex construction on the whole original word. It has a\n   fresh orthogonal innovation at each large bridge and negative\n   correlations between successive constant-gradient H_2 blocks.\n   The coefficients may vary arbitrarily with the physical bridge.\n   The family even permits negative backward coefficients below-1;\n   legality uses their actual even products, not a unit coefficient cap.\n   All original queries, remote pairs, values and the true initial\n   distance are proved. But its ENTIRE declared family satisfies\n\n       V <= (5/9)^(2^(m-3)-1).                              (1)\n\n   This is uniform over its terminal parameter and every permitted\n   variable coefficient. Consequently its payoff divided by complete\n   same-word K, complete same-word ray, or the UNaugmented full complex\n   channel tends to zero. Its original-B normalized payoff also tends\n   to zero. These comparisons already hold strictly for m>=9.\n\n3. Removing the reserve growth in that rule by the exact constant\n   choice kappa=s+1 is impossible. The ORIGINAL physical pair(3,8)\n   has a positive two-cycle, including when eta=1. Reassigning values\n   cannot fix this proposed repair. Intermediate uncapped policies\n   are NOT excluded by this statement.\n\nThe signed construction is not a common added gradient, a fixed\ntriangle, a finite orthogonal sum of such triangles, or the prior\nnonnegative one-tail law. Its positive correlations inside each H_2\nblock also violate the nonpositive-correlation hypothesis of C100.\nAt the beginning of the final H_2 block its support and value reserve\nexceed the short-step caps used in C98/C99. Those unverified drafts\nare recalled only to identify the changed hypotheses.\n\n## 2. An explicit nested-normal geometry and all its reverse constraints\n\nFirst take an arbitrary word with h_i>1 and eta>1. Write\n\n    tau=eta-1,\n    Ttail_i=tau+sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t,       0<=i<=n,\n    d_i=h_i-1.\n\nChoose finite numbers Ldual_i with\n\n    Ldual_0>=...>=Ldual_n=1.\n\nThese are inputs, not unknown feasible Gram entries. In the orthonormal\nbasis e_0,...,e_n set\n\n    rdual_n=e_n,\n    rdual_i=rdual_(i+1)+sqrt(Ldual_i-Ldual_(i+1))*e_i,\n    delta_i=Ttail_i/Ldual_i,\n    y_i=delta_i*rdual_i,\n    x_n=eta*e_n, g_n=e_n,\n    g_i=(y_i-x_(i+1))/d_i,  x_i=y_i+g_i,       i=n-1,...,0. (2)\n\nEvery vector is real. The physical update is literal:\nx_i-h_i*g_i=y_i-d_i*g_i=x_(i+1). The dependence of g_i includes\nthe full future position and the independent accumulated dual normal;\nit is not a scalar multiple of g_(i+1) plus a fresh innovation.\n\nInductively, ||rdual_i||^2=Ldual_i and\n\n    <rdual_i,g_j>=1                 for every j>=i.          (3)\n\nIndeed rdual_i differs from rdual_(i+1) by a vector orthogonal to\nthe whole suffix. Thus it pairs to1 with every already constructed\nfuture gradient, and\n\n    <rdual_i,x_(i+1)>=eta+sum_(t>i)h_t=Ttail_i-d_i.\n\nUsing delta_i*Ldual_i=Ttail_i in(2) then proves(3) also for j=i.\nIn particular <y_i,g_i>=delta_i>=0. Prescribe formal values\n\n    f_i=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2,\n    x_*=g_*=f_*=0.\n\nDirect expansion in the accepted convention gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                   (4)\n\nThus Q_(j,i)=0 for every i<j, Q_(i,*)=0, and\nQ_(*,i)=-delta_i. The reverse rows remain necessary and have NOT\nbeen removed.\n\nThere is an exact scalar form of every remaining row. Put\n\n    Adual_i=1/Ldual_i,  Delta_i=Adual_(i+1)-Adual_i>=0,\n    Epair_(j,i)=Adual_j*(1-<rdual_j,g_i>),    i<j,\n    Epair_(j,j)=0.\n\nExpanding x_(i+1) as eta*g_n+sum_(t>i)h_t*g_t gives\n\n    d_i*Epair_(j,i)\n       =Ttail_i*(Adual_j-Adual_i)\n          -sum_(k=i+1)^(j-1) h_k*Epair_(j,k).              (5)\n\nThis retains every earlier/future pair. In particular\n\n    Epair_(2,1)=Ttail_1*Delta_1/d_1,\n    Epair_(2,0)=[Ttail_0*Delta_0\n                    +(h_0-Ttail_1/d_1)*Delta_1]/d_0,\n    Q_(0,2)=-Ttail_2*Epair_(2,0).                          (6)\n\nEquations(2)--(6) are an explicit geometric audit, not a promise that\narbitrary Ldual satisfies interpolation. They are not offered as a\nfree feasibility oracle for the main problem.\n\n## 3. The entire positive-power policy fails an actual cycle\n\nThe natural concrete policy is\n\n    Ldual_i=(Ttail_i/tau)^p,       p>0.                     (7)\n\nIt satisfies the required monotonicity, so the real-vector construction\nexists for every p>0. It also includes horizon-dependent p and eta.\nNevertheless it is NOT an interpolable trajectory on the original H_m.\n\nThe first two physical steps are\n\n    u=h_0=1+a,  v=h_1=1+beta,  d_0=a, d_1=beta.\n\nLet P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and Ttail_0=T. As a function of P, the inverse\nnormal size in(7) is Adual(P)=tau^p*(T-P)^(-p). Its derivative is\npositive and strictly increasing on these intervals. Therefore\n\n    Delta_0/u < Delta_1/v,       Delta_1>0.                (8)\n\nMore generally the non-strict version of(8), with Delta_1>0, holds\nfor any convex increasing profile on the first two physical intervals.\n\nSubstitute Delta_0<=u*Delta_1/v and Ttail_0=Ttail_1+u into(6):\n\n    Q_(0,2) >= [Ttail_2*Delta_1/(a*beta*v)]\n                 *[(v-u*beta)*Ttail_1-u*beta*(u+v)].        (9)\n\nEvery factor outside the bracket is positive. For m>=4,\na<1/2, beta<1, u<3/2, v<2, and Ttail_1>n-1>=14. Hence\n\n    v-u*beta=1-a*beta>1/2,\n    u*beta*(u+v)<21/4,\n    (v-u*beta)*Ttail_1-u*beta*(u+v)>7/4>0.                 (10)\n\nThus Q_(0,2)>0 for every policy in(7), without any restriction on\nthe growth of p or eta with m. From(3),(4), Q_(2,1)=Q_(1,0)=0.\nConsequently\n\n                    Q_(0,2)+Q_(2,1)+Q_(1,0)>0.           (11)\n\nThe function-value terms cancel from this sum. Changing ALL sampled\nvalues, choosing a different minimum value, or moving a minimizer\ntherefore cannot make these same points and gradients smoothly\nconvexly interpolable. It is an actual three-point cyclic failure,\nnot merely an inconvenient choice of projection-conjugate heights.\n\nThe p=0 boundary has Ldual_i=1 and g_i=e_n at every sample, as follows\nby induction in(2). It is the constant-gradient Huber channel and\nhas no positive increment in(8). Nonconvex profiles of Adual, including\nprofiles with strongly unequal early increments, remain outside(9)'s\nexclusion. The claim is not that all nested-normal geometries fail.\n\nHere is the reason for trying a block repair next. In a one-tail\nprojection extension write g_i=-(kappa/d_i)g_(i+1)+v_i*e_i and force\n<y_i,g_(i+1)-g_i>=0. The identities are\n\n    delta_i=delta_(i+1)-(kappa-1)*||g_(i+1)||^2,\n    v_i^2=(d_i+kappa)*delta_i/d_i^2,\n    y_i=y_(i+1)+(1-kappa)g_(i+1)+d_i*v_i*e_i.             (12)\n\nIf kappa>1 and the next normal is also tied to a future gradient g_j\nwith <g_(i+1),g_j><||g_(i+1)||^2, then\n\n    <y_i,g_j-g_i>\n       =(1-kappa)*(<g_(i+1),g_j>-||g_(i+1)||^2)>0.\n\nThis diagnoses the unpriced remote row in the naive strong-negative\nstep. The next construction puts an ACTUAL H_2 holding block after\neach bridge, retains its whole mass, and audits the resulting rows.\n\n## 4. A legal signed law on every actual H_2 block and larger bridge\n\nFor the original word let\n\n    s=sum H_2=3+2a+beta,\n    Ccap=(s+1)/2,\n    Nphase=2^(m-2)-1,\n    Lcount=2^(m-3).\n\nThere are Nphase bridges and Nphase+1 blocks in the exact decomposition\n\n    H_m=(H_2,b_0,H_2,b_1,...,b_(Nphase-1),H_2).\n\nThe bridge at physical index4j+3 is\n\n    b_j=1+d_j,\n    d_j=a*c^(2+nu_2(j+1)),       0<=j<Nphase,              (13)\n\nwhere nu_2(k) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive integer k.\nNo word has been replaced by a compressed algorithm: the individual\nqueries inside each H_2 are supplied explicitly below. Formula(13)\nuses the SAME enclosing a,c even in the smallest blocks.\n\nChoose ANY eta>=1 and ANY sequence\n\n    0<=kappa_j<=Ccap,       0<=j<Nphase.\n\nThese may depend on m and on each physical bridge separately. Put\nrhold_j=s+1-kappa_j, so rhold_j>=Ccap>=kappa_j. Use the orthonormal\nbasis e_0,...,e_(Nphase). At the end of the last block set\n\n    G_(Nphase)=e_(Nphase),\n    Y_(Nphase)=(eta-1)*e_(Nphase),\n    X_(Nphase)=eta*e_(Nphase),\n    q_(Nphase)=1, delta_(Nphase)=eta-1,\n    Znormal_(Nphase)=(eta-1)^2.\n\nConstruct backward for j=Nphase-1,...,0 by\n\n    delta_j=delta_(j+1)+rhold_j*q_(j+1),\n    v_j=sqrt((d_j+kappa_j)*delta_j)/d_j,\n    t_j=-kappa_j/d_j,\n    G_j=t_j*G_(j+1)+v_j*e_j,\n    Y_j=Y_(j+1)+rhold_j*G_(j+1)+d_j*v_j*e_j,\n    X_j=Y_j+G_j.                                         (14)\n\nHere q_j=||G_j||^2 and Znormal_j=||Y_j||^2; their completely specified\nscalar recurrences are\n\n    q_j=[kappa_j^2*q_(j+1)+(d_j+kappa_j)*delta_j]/d_j^2,\n    Znormal_j=Znormal_(j+1)+2*rhold_j*delta_(j+1)\n                  +rhold_j^2*q_(j+1)+(d_j+kappa_j)*delta_j. (15)\n\nThe quantities under the square roots are positive: q_(j+1)>0 and\nrhold_j>0 imply delta_j>0. The vectors therefore realize a PSD Gram\nmatrix in dimension Nphase+1. Direct multiplication gives\n\n    <Y_j,G_j>=delta_j,\n    <Y_j,G_(j+1)>=delta_j,\n    X_j=X_(j+1)+s*G_(j+1)+b_j*G_j.                        (16)\n\nFor example the first identity uses\nt_j*delta_j+d_j*v_j^2=delta_j. For the third, the coefficient identity\nrhold_j+t_j=s+1+b_j*t_j and d_j+1=b_j suffice.\n\nWrite H_2=(u,v,u), where u=1+a and v=1+beta. Define remaining\nblock masses a_rem,0=s, a_rem,1=u+v, a_rem,2=u, a_rem,3=0.\nFor every phase j and r=0,1,2,3 provide the ORIGINAL physical sample\n\n    x_(4j+r)=X_j+a_rem,r*G_j,\n    g_(4j+r)=G_j,\n    f_(4j+r)=delta_j+(a_rem,r+1/2)*q_j.                   (17)\n\nThe last sample4*Nphase+3 is precisely n=2^m-1. Within each block\nsubtracting its next physical step decreases a_rem by that step.\nAt its bridge, (16) gives\n\n    x_(4j+3)-b_j*G_j=X_(j+1)+s*G_(j+1)=x_(4j+4).\n\nThus every physical update is correct. Different phases have distinct\ngradients because v_j>0 gives a fresh nonzero coordinate. For strictly\npositive kappa_j the adjacent phases have negative inner products;\nwithin each physical H_2 block the correlations are positive.\n\n## 5. All pairs, every intermediate query, and the one static function\n\nTwo estimates prove all the normal inequalities. First,\n\n    q_j >= |t_j|*q_(j+1).                                (18)\n\nIndeed, using delta_j>=rhold_j*q_(j+1) and rhold_j>=kappa_j,\nthe numerator of q_j-(kappa_j/d_j)*q_(j+1) is at least\n\n    [kappa_j^2-kappa_j*d_j+(d_j+kappa_j)*kappa_j]\n          *q_(j+1)=2*kappa_j^2*q_(j+1)>=0.\n\nFresh innovation orthogonality gives, for i<j,\n\n    <G_i,G_j>=Ptail_(i,j)*q_j,\n    Ptail_(i,j)=product_(k=i)^(j-1)t_k.                    (19)\n\nIterating(18) shows |<G_i,G_j>|<=q_i. This holds even when an individual\nbackward coefficient has magnitude greater than1.\n\nSecond, EVERY Ptail_(i,j)<=1. A negative product already has this\nproperty. For an even number of factors, pair consecutive bridge\npositions. In the ruler in(13) every adjacent pair has one level2\nand one level at least3. Write d_low=a*c^2=beta*c and\nd_next=a*c^3=c*d_low. For m>=4,\n\n    d_low=lambda*(1-1/m^2)>2,\n    c>12/5,\n    d_low*d_next=c*d_low^2>48/5>9>Ccap^2.                 (20)\n\nHere Ccap<3 follows from a<1/2,beta<1. Each paired absolute product\nis at most Ccap^2/(d_low*d_next)<1. This proves the second estimate\nwithout requiring |t_j|<=1 at every bridge.\n\nAt a phase end, all future normals hold by backward induction.\nThe next comparison is tied by(16). For k>j+1, subtract that tie:\n\n    <Y_j,G_k-G_j>\n      =<Y_(j+1),G_k-G_(j+1)>\n          +rhold_j*<G_(j+1),G_k-G_(j+1)> <=0.             (21)\n\nThe first term is the induction hypothesis and the second is\nnonpositive by(18),(19). At an intermediate query in this phase,\nthe normal is Y_j+w*G_j, with 0<=w<=s. Its future comparisons add\nw*<G_j,G_k-G_j><=0, so they also hold.\n\nFor a past phase i<j, write G_i=Ptail_(i,j)*G_j+z, with z in\nspan{e_i,...,e_(j-1)}. Both Y_j and G_j are orthogonal to z. Therefore\nthe corresponding normal comparison at EVERY query in phase j is\n\n    <Y_j+w*G_j,G_i-G_j>\n       =(Ptail_(i,j)-1)*(delta_j+w*q_j)<=0.               (22)\n\nSame-phase comparisons are zero. The comparison with the zero\ngradient is -(delta_j+w*q_j)<=0. Equations(21),(22) therefore cover\nALL original pairs, including pairs in remote recursive children,\nand every intermediate holding query.\n\nNow define a SINGLE function for the whole word by\n\n    Cblock=conv{0,G_0,...,G_(Nphase)},\n    Fblock(x)=max_(z in Cblock){<x,z>-||z||^2/2}.           (23)\n\nCompleting the square identifies the maximizer with the Euclidean\nprojection P_Cblock(x). The proved normal inequalities imply\nP_Cblock(x_(4j+r))=G_j at each sample in(17).\n\nFor completeness, the projection inequalities at two points x,z give\n<P(x)-P(z),x-z> >= ||P(x)-P(z)||^2. Hence P is1-Lipschitz.\nComparing the two maximizing affine functions in(23) gives\n\n    <P(x),u> <= Fblock(x+u)-Fblock(x) <= <P(x+u),u>.\n\nThe difference from the left expression is between0 and ||u||^2.\nThus Fblock is differentiable with gradient P, and is convex and\neverywhere finite. Its gradient is1-Lipschitz. Its values at the\nsamples are precisely(17).\n\nUsing(4), every physical Q equals the proved normal comparison.\nIn particular all optimum rows are retained: Q_(i,*)=0 and\nQ_(*,i)=-(delta_j+a_rem,r*q_j)<=0. There is no deletion of an\nincoming terminal row, an old query, or a cross-block pair.\n\n## 6. Exact minimum set and paid radius\n\nThe minimum value of(23) is0 and its ENTIRE minimum set is\n\n    Cblock^polar={z:<z,G_j><=0 for every j}.                (24)\n\nFor z in that cone every maximand is nonpositive, with value0 at\nthe zero vertex. If z is outside the cone, some <z,G_j>>0; a\nsufficiently small positive multiple of that vertex has positive\nmaximand. This proves(24) in both directions.\n\nThe initial point telescopes from the actual physical updates:\n\n    x_0=eta*G_(Nphase)+sum_(i<n)h_i*g_i.\n\nIt is a positive combination of gradient vertices. Hence <x_0,z><=0\nfor every z in(24), and\n\n    ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2,\n    dist(x_0,argmin Fblock)^2=||x_0||^2.\n\nThe exact radius and terminal payoff are therefore\n\n    Rblock0_sq=Znormal_0+2*(s+1)*delta_0+(s+1)^2*q_0,\n    Vblock=(2eta-1)/Rblock0_sq.                            (25)\n\nEquations(14),(15),(25) are finite explicit scalar and vector laws;\nno unknown feasible matrix or function-value optimization is hidden.\nThe state Znormal is a squared normal length, NOT the ambient\ndimension. Multiplying Fblock by any L>0 and using h_i/L preserves\nthe entire trajectory and normalized payoff.\n\nIsometric embeddings leave the radius unchanged. A finite orthogonal\nsum of these functions has a product minimum set and payoff equal\nto a radius-squared-weighted average of its component payoffs. The\nbound below consequently covers these embeddings and finite sums too.\n\nThe extreme allowed constant choice kappa_j=Ccap demonstrates that\nthe construction genuinely goes beyond unit-magnitude backward tails.\nFor m>=4, beta>=3/4 and c<25/8 imply a>6/25, whence\nCcap>2+3/8+6/25>5/2>d_low. Thus t_j<-1 at every level2 bridge.\nEquations(18)--(22), not an unproved correlation heuristic, ensure\nthat this choice is legal.\n\n## 7. Uniform obstruction for the whole declared signed family\n\nThe price is forced by the repeated low bridges. There are exactly\nLcount=2^(m-3) level2 bridges, at even phase indices\nj=0,2,...,2*Lcount-2, and Nphase=2*Lcount-1.\n\nAll delta_j>=delta_(j+1). At every level2 bridge,\n\n    q_j >= delta_j/d_low.\n\nIf j>=2, the preceding phase transition then satisfies\n\n    delta_(j-1)=delta_j+rhold_(j-1)*q_j\n       >= (1+Ccap/d_low)*delta_j.\n\nThe last bridge is level2 and\ndelta_(Nphase-1)>=eta-1+Ccap. Iterating the previous inequality\nacross all but the first low bridge, retaining the intervening\nnondecreases, gives\n\n    delta_0 >= (1+Ccap/d_low)^(Lcount-1)*(eta-1+Ccap).\n\nSince Ccap>2 and d_low<lambda<5/2,\n\n    delta_0 >= (9/5)^(Lcount-1)*(eta-1+Ccap).              (26)\n\nThe exact radius(25) is greater than2*delta_0. With tau=eta-1>=0,\n\n    Vblock <= (5/9)^(Lcount-1)*(2tau+1)/(2*(tau+Ccap))\n           <= (5/9)^(Lcount-1).                           (27)\n\nThis proves(1) uniformly over EVERY coefficient sequence in the\nstated interval, even when all parameters vary with m. It is not\na claim about every possible signed projection geometry.\n\n## 8. Complete same-word comparisons and ORIGINAL-B normalization\n\nLet Huber_m=1/(1+2S_m). The complete single-cut channel from accepted\nb98bb807d514e80d is\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),\n       max_(j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2\n                                      *(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]}.\n\nThe complete ray channel F_ray_n from accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a retains\nALL feasible ordered checkpoint subsets, including the empty subset\nand every single checkpoint, with its remote offsets, accumulated\nareas, and full terminal maximization. Consequently\n\n    Huber_m <= K_n(H_m) <= F_ray_n(H_m).                   (28)\n\nNo particular checkpoint subset is substituted for the full ray.\nIt is enough for the comparison that its complete maximum includes\nthese admissible witnesses; equality on H_m is not asserted.\n\nFor the full complex channel use exactly the objects in accepted\ndfde2fa326c908bf: Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t*z), points xi_i=Poly_i(z),\ngradients gamma_i=z*Poly_i(z), and the zero optimum sample. The\nlegal set D_h retains every directed simple cycle C, including\ntwo-cycles, and obeys\n\n    (Re z-|z|^2)*Energy_C >= 2*|Im z*Area_C|.\n\nHere Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2 and\nArea_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)*xi_i). The edge weight is\n<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2; ell_n is the maximum\nweight of a directed path n->*; Gap_h(z)=-2ell_n. Write\n\n    Cfull(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z).\n\nThis is the unaugmented FULL schedule-dependent channel with initial\npoint1 and specified minimizer0. It is not the universal lens.\n\nThe following elementary same-word lower bound is useful:\n\n                   Cfull(h)>=1/[2*(1+2sum h)].            (29)\n\nTo prove it for any nonnegative word of mass S, let theta=1/(2S+1)\nand P=product_(i<n)(1-h_i*theta)>0. Real theta belongs to the full\nD_h. Every scalar queried point is at least P. A single function\nhas continuous nondecreasing1-Lipschitz derivative\n\n    F'(x)=x          for x<=theta*P,\n          theta*P    for theta*P<=x<=P,\n          theta*x    for x>=P,\n    F(0)=0.\n\nIt has unique minimizer0 and realizes all those points and gradients.\nIn the complex plane one may add (Im x)^2/2 to F(Re x).\nIntegration gives normalized terminal gap\n(2theta-theta^2)*P^2. By elementary multiplication of nonnegative\nfactors, P>=1-S*theta=(1+theta)/2. Thus this legal gap is at least\ntheta/2, because\n(2-theta)*(1+theta)^2=2+3theta-theta^3>=2 for0<=theta<=1.\nThis proves(29), without assuming that Cfull dominates Huber itself.\n\nWrite Ucap_m=(5/9)^(Lcount-1). Equations(27)--(29) imply\n\n    Vblock/K_n(H_m) <= (1+2S_m)*Ucap_m,\n    Vblock/F_ray_n(H_m) <= (1+2S_m)*Ucap_m,\n    Vblock/Cfull(H_m) <= 2*(1+2S_m)*Ucap_m.                (30)\n\nThe exact physical mass is\n\n    1+S_m=(1-A_mass)*2^m+A_mass*c^m,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2).                                      (31)\n\nThis follows by summing the2^(m-1-j) occurrences of1+a*c^j.\nSince c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1 and beta<1, one has0<A_mass<1.\nConsequently1+S_m<=c^m<4^m for m>=4. Every right-hand side of(30)\ntends to zero, uniformly over the entire signed family.\n\nFor a fully explicit finite comparison, (5/9)^2<1/2 implies\nUcap_m<2^(-(Lcount-1)/2). For all m>=9,\nLcount=2^(m-3)>=4m+5; this starts at m=9 and persists by doubling.\nUsing1+2S_m<2^(2m+1) shows the last right-hand side of(30) is\nstrictly below1. Therefore for every m>=9 every member is strictly\nbelow Huber, complete K/ray, and the UNaugmented full complex channel.\n\nFor clarity the benchmark here is the ORIGINAL all-split recurrence:\n\n    C_0^bench=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    Gbench(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n^bench=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k^bench,C_(n-k-1)^bench),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)Gbench(C_k^bench,B_(n-k-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nFor EACH constructed member the exact original normalization is\n\n    B_(2^m-1)*Vblock\n      =B_(2^m-1)*(2eta-1)\n          /[Znormal_0+2*(s+1)*delta_0+(s+1)^2*q_0].        (32)\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Hence\n\n    0<=B_(2^m-1)*Vblock <= (1+4*3^m)*Ucap_m ->0.          (33)\n\nNo balanced-tree surrogate or changed enclosing parameter has been\nused. The supremum over the declared family also has limit0 in(33).\nThis is a weakness of these adversaries, not an upper bound on W.\n\n## 9. The zero-growth signed repair fails physical pair(3,8)\n\nThe bound(26) suggests increasing kappa until rhold=0. Test precisely\n\n                 kappa_j=s+1=:kappa_full  for every j.     (34)\n\nApply the SAME explicit recurrences(14),(15) without the cap. The\nradicands are still nonnegative: delta_j=eta-1=tau for every phase,\nand v_j^2=(d_j+kappa_full)*tau/d_j^2. The physical recursions also\nstill hold. Thus this is real PSD data, including tau=0, but it is\nnot an interpolable gradient trajectory.\n\nSince rhold=0, the end normals satisfy\nY_j=Y_(j+1)+d_j*v_j*e_j. They pair to tau with EVERY future gradient.\nMeanwhile the first two bridge coefficients give\n\n    G_0=Pbad*G_2+z, z in span{e_0,e_1},\n    Pbad=kappa_full^2/(d_low*d_next)>1.                   (35)\n\nTo check the strict sign on the original parameters, for m>=4\none has a>6/25,beta>=3/4, so s+1>5. Also\nc<25/8 and d_low<5/2 imply\nd_low*d_next=c*d_low^2<625/32<25<(s+1)^2.\n\nThe physical index3 is the END of phase0, while index8 is the START\nof phase2. Its normal is Y_2+s*G_2, and its gradient is G_2. The\nnormal at3 is Y_0, with gradient G_0. The original residuals are\n\n    Q_(3,8)=(Pbad-1)*(tau+s*q_2)>0,\n    Q_(8,3)=<Y_0,G_2-G_0>=0.                             (36)\n\nThe strict positivity includes eta=1: then q_2>0 because the\nnonzero scalar backward coefficients preserve the nonzero terminal\ngradient, and the actual phase-start mass s is positive. Using only\nphase ends would miss this failure at eta=1.\n\nThe two-cycle sum in(36) is independent of values. Even freely\nreassigning the values or changing the optimum cannot repair these\nsame points and gradients. We do not claim that all other rows pass,\nnor that every choice Ccap<kappa_j<s+1 fails. Formula(36) closes the\nspecified zero-growth proposal, not that entire remaining interval.\n\n## 10. Adversarial checks, literature, and exact remaining scope\n\nThe geometric proofs retain every physical query inside each H_2,\nboth orientations of all cross-block pairs, the optimum, and the\nactual polar minimum set. Individual backward coefficients<-1 are\npermitted and explicitly checked. The legal-family estimate is\nuniform in arbitrary variable capped kappa_j and arbitrary eta>=1,\nincluding parameters depending on m and finite orthogonal sums.\n\nThe power obstruction does not rely on a fixed exponent, large-tail\napproximation, numerical sign test, or a prescribed value assignment:\n(9)--(11) are exact and hold uniformly. The signed zero-growth failure\nuses physical(3,8), not a compressed phase model. Its eta=1 boundary\nrequires the intermediate holding query and is not silently omitted.\n\nThe new legal family is NOT all signed Markov laws. It retains a\nradial terminal point, projection-conjugate values, constant gradient\ninside each original H_2, and the concrete backward state(14).\nThe sufficient cap kappa_j<=Ccap is not asserted necessary. General\nuncapped changing kappa, correlated innovations involving two future\nmodes, changed conjugate heights together with changed gradients, and\nnonconvex inverse-normal profiles remain outside the closed scopes.\n\nIn particular, the three findings do not close the full nested-normal\ncone, all multistage correlated constructions, or unrestricted W.\nThey neither extend accepted finite H6 to new enclosing parameters\nnor contradict its fixed finite payoff. The main Goal remains OPEN.\nEven an eventual competitive construction on H_m would still require\nthe original uniform all-n/all-nonnegative-word bridge.\n\nLiterature retrieval was program-conditioned: the active program was\nan explicit changing correlated law with full-history interpolation\nand paid radius, and the search mode was mutation. Statement and original benchmark\n\nFor a nonnegative integer n and a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h)\nis the supremum of\n\n  2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)\n\nover every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable f\nwith L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). All-split comparison with primitive capacities\n\nWe prove jointly that\n\n  G(a,lambda d)<=lambda Omega(a,d)   for all a,d>=0.       (6)\n\nWhen a=d=0 both sides are zero. Otherwise put w=Omega(a,d)>0 and t=a/w.\nThe equation w^2-(a+d)w-ad=0 gives\n\n  0<=t<=1,    d/w=(1-t)/(1+t).\n\nThe larger root of P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda d)v+4a^2 is G(a,lambda d).\nSince lambda^2=2lambda+1, substitution and expansion give\n\n  P(lambda w)/w^2\n    =lambda^2-4lambda t-lambda^2*(1-t)/(1+t)+4t^2\n    =4t*(t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t) >=0.\n\nAlso w>=a+d, so lambda w>=lambda(a+d)>=2a+lambda d/2,\nthe vertex of P. A quadratic of positive leading coefficient is\nnonnegative to the right of its vertex only at or beyond its larger\nroot.
